<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Let <i>K</i> range over totally real quartic fields with no proper intermediate field, and let $`\mathcal O`$ be any order in <i>K</i>. We prove that the packets of periodic diagonal orbits attached to invertible $`\mathcal O`$-ideal classes, weighted by orbit volume, equidistribute with no escape of mass as $`|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}\nolimits (\mathcal O)|`$ tends to infinity. The proof combines measure rigidity with a cubic-resolvent estimate that remains uniform at primes dividing the order index.

- **claim-packet-equidistribution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $(K_i,\mathcal O_i,\sigma_i)$ be any sequence in which $K_i$ is a primitive totally real quartic field, $\mathcal O_i$ is an order in $K_i$, and $\sigma_i$ is an ordering of its real embeddings. If $\|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(\mathcal O_i)\|\to\infty$, then $$\int_{X_4} f\,d\mu_{\mathcal O_i,\sigma_i} \longrightarrow \int_{X_4} f\,dm_4 \qquad\text{for every }f\in C_c(X_4).$$ There is no escape of mass: for every $\varepsilon>0$ there is a compact $\mathcal K\subset X_4$ such that $\mu_{\mathcal O_i,\sigma_i}(\mathcal K)\ge1-\varepsilon$ for all sufficiently large $i$.
  - Explanation: Complete volume-weighted packets of invertible ideal classes converge to Haar probability as the order discriminant grows, with no escape of mass.
- **claim-finite-local-labels** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-pkt-local-labels)): The set $\mathscr L_{\mathcal O}(I)$ is finite, and its cardinality is $$\label{eq:pkt-label-count} N_{\mathcal O}=\prod_{p\mid q}[R_p^\times:\mathcal O_p^\times],$$ independently of $I$. Its members satisfy $$[I:L]=q,\qquad RL=I,$$ and are proper invertible fractional $\mathcal O$-ideals. Every ordinary class of proper invertible fractional $\mathcal O$-ideals is represented, up to multiplication by $K^\times$, by a member of one of the sets $\mathscr L_{\mathcal O}(I)$. Uniform probability on $\mathscr L_{\mathcal O}(I)$ is induced by independent normalized Haar probabilities for $t_p\in R_p^\times$.
  - Explanation: Local unit translates give finitely many labels above each maximal-order ideal class, represent all invertible order classes, and carry the required uniform probability.
- **claim-packet-model-volume** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-pkt-model)): The maps $$(L,v)\longmapsto c_I\sigma(L)v \qquad (L\in\mathscr L_{\mathcal O}(I),\ v\in T_\infty)$$ give a bijection from the disjoint union over the chosen $I$ of the quotients by $$\label{eq:pkt-unit-action} \epsilon:(L,v)\longmapsto (\epsilon L,\sigma(\epsilon)^{-1}v),\qquad \epsilon\in U,$$ onto $\bigcup_{\mathcal Y\in\mathcal P_{\mathcal O,\sigma}}\mathcal Y\subset X_4$. Counting measure on the $L$’s times $dv$ descends to the sum of the Haar measures of those orbits. In particular, $$\label{eq:pkt-total-volume} \sum_{\mathcal Y\in\mathcal P_{\mathcal O,\sigma}}\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(\mathcal Y) =N_{\mathcal O}\sqrt D\,\kappa .$$ Writing $\mu_*=\mu_{\mathcal O,\sigma}$, for every nonnegative Borel function $\Phi$ on $X_4$ one has $$\label{eq:pkt-integration} \int_{X_4}\Phi\,d\mu_* =\frac{1}{h_KN_{\mathcal O}\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(\mathscr F_U)} \sum_I\sum_{L\in\mathscr L_{\mathcal O}(I)} \int_{\mathscr F_U}\Phi(c_I\sigma(L)v)\,dv .$$ The identity permits infinite values on both sides.
  - Explanation: Quotienting the local labels and real parameters by maximal-order units identifies the packet and gives its exact volume and integration formula.
- **claim-ordinary-vector-unfolding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-ord-unfolding)): For every nonnegative Borel $f$ on $\mathbb R^4$, $$\label{eq:ord-unfolding} \int_{X_4}E_f\,d\mu_* =\frac1{\sqrt D\,\kappa} \sum_{\substack{\mathfrak a\subset R\\\mathfrak a\ne0}} P(\mathfrak a)\, \mathcal V_f\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak a/X\bigr).$$ Both sides are allowed to be infinite.
  - Explanation: The packet average of a nonnegative vector sum equals a weighted integral-ideal sum with the zeta-residue normalization retained.
- **claim-local-shell-mass-moment-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ord-shells)): With the preceding notation, $$\label{eq:ord-shell-mass} \sum_{\boldsymbol l}\frac{P(\boldsymbol l)}{d(\boldsymbol l)} =\frac{Z_q}{q}.$$ For every $\eta>0$ there is a constant $C_\eta$, independent of $K,\mathcal O$, such that $$\label{eq:ord-shell-moment} \sum_{\boldsymbol l} P(\boldsymbol l)d(\boldsymbol l)^{-1+1/8} \le C_\eta\,\frac{q^\eta}{q}.$$ In particular there is an absolute constant $C$ for which $$\label{eq:ord-shell-tail} \sum_{d(\boldsymbol l)>X^{1/8}} \frac{P(\boldsymbol l)}{d(\boldsymbol l)} \le \frac Cq X^{-1/128}.$$
  - Explanation: An exact shell-mass identity and a uniform positive moment control the large-norm tail even for orders of large index.
- **claim-residue-prime-sum-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ord-residue)): There are absolute constants $D_0>1$, $h_0>0$, $Y_0>1$, and $C\ge1$ with the following properties for every primitive totally real quartic field $K$.* 1. *If $D\ge D_0$, then $$\label{eq:ord-residue-comparison} C^{-1}\kappa\le h\zeta_K(1+h)\le C\kappa \qquad(0<h\le4/\log D).$$ If $D<D_0$, the same inequalities hold for $0<h\le h_0$.* 2. *One has $$\label{eq:ord-residue-lower} \kappa^{-1}\le C\log(3D).$$* 3. *Whenever $Y\ge\max\{Y_0,D^{1/4}\}$, $$\label{eq:ord-prime-sum} \exp\left(\sum_{p\le Y}\frac{a_K(p)}p\right) \le C\kappa\log Y .$$* *The sum in Equation <a href="#eq:ord-prime-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ord-prime-sum">[eq:ord-prime-sum]</a> is over rational primes.
  - Explanation: Primitivity supplies uniform near-pole residue comparisons, a lower bound for the residue, and the prime-sum estimate needed in both counts.
- **claim-uniform-shiu-interval** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ord-shiu)): There are absolute constants $C,u_0$ such that, for every nonnegative multiplicative function $b$ with $b(n)\le d_4(n)$, every real $u\ge u_0$, and every $u^{1/2}\le v\le u$, $$\label{eq:ord-shiu} \sum_{u-v<n\le u}b(n) \le C\frac v{\log u} \exp\left(\sum_{p\le u}\frac{b(p)}p\right).$$
  - Explanation: A divisor-dominated nonnegative multiplicative function satisfies a uniform short-interval upper bound.
- **claim-weighted-ideal-intervals** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-ord-weighted-intervals)): There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for each fixed $A\ge1$ and $c>0$, there is a threshold $X_0(A,c)$ with the following property. For every $K,\mathcal O,\sigma$ under consideration with $X\ge X_0(A,c)$, and all real $x,y$ satisfying $$X^{3/4}\le x\le AX,\qquad cx\le y\le x,$$ one has $$\label{eq:ord-weighted-interval} \sum_{\substack{\mathfrak a\subset R,\ \mathfrak a\ne0\\ x-y<\mathop{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak a\le x}}P(\mathfrak a) \le C\,\frac{\kappa y}{q}.$$ The constant $C$ is independent of $A,c$; the threshold may depend on them.
  - Explanation: Separating conductor-supported shells from coprime ideals preserves the residue and inverse order-index factors in norm intervals.
- **claim-packet-tightness** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-ord-tightness)): There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for every fixed $0<r<1$, $$\label{eq:ord-cusp} \limsup_{X\longrightarrow\infty} \mu_*\{\Lambda:\text{some }0\ne v\in\Lambda \text{ satisfies }\\|v\\|_\infty<r\} \le Cr^4 .$$ The limit superior ranges over all primitive totally real quartic fields, all their orders, and all orderings of their real embeddings. Consequently, for every sequence of these packet measures $\mu_i=\mu_{\mathcal O_i,\sigma_i}$ with $X_i\to\infty$ and every $\epsilon>0$, there is a compact $\mathcal K_\epsilon\subset X_4$ with $\mu_i(\mathcal K_\epsilon)\ge1-\epsilon$ for all sufficiently large $i$. Such a sequence is tight, and every weak subsequential limit is an $A_4$-invariant probability on $X_4$.
  - Explanation: The short-vector count controls cusp mass and makes packet sequences tight, with invariant probability subsequential limits.
- **claim-vector-box-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ord-boxes)): Let $V_0\subset(\mathbb R^\times)^4$ be compact, and let $b>0$. There are constants $C,r_0>0$, depending only on $V_0,b$, such that for every fixed $0<r<r_0$ there is a threshold $X_0(V_0,b,r)$ for which $$\label{eq:ord-vector-box} \int_{X_4}E_{\mathbf 1_{v+[-br,br]^4}}\,d\mu_* \le Cr^4\qquad(v\in V_0,\ X\ge X_0(V_0,b,r)).$$ The threshold and constants are uniform over $K,\mathcal O,\sigma$ and over the centers $v\in V_0$.
  - Explanation: Boxes centered in a compact set away from coordinate hyperplanes have uniformly bounded packet vector averages; the discriminant threshold may depend on the fixed radius.
- **claim-limit-compact-ball-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-ord-balls)): Let $\mu$ be a weak probability limit of packet measures along $X_i\to\infty$. For every compact $\Omega\subset X_4$, there are constants $C_\Omega,r_\Omega>0$ such that $$\label{eq:ord-balls} \mu(xB(r))\le C_\Omega r^4 \qquad(x\in\Omega,\ 0<r<r_\Omega).$$
  - Explanation: Every packet probability limit satisfies a local ball-mass estimate uniformly over compact sets of centers.
- **claim-two-sided-tube-entropy** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-rig-tube-criterion)): Let $\xi_i$ and $\xi$ be $a(\mathbb R)$-invariant Borel probabilities on $X_4$, with $\xi_i$ converging weak-\* to $\xi$. Suppose that $t_i>0$ tend to infinity and that $\eta>0$. Assume that for every compact $\Omega\subset X_4$ there are an open identity neighborhood $B\subset\mathrm{SL}_4(\mathbb R)$ and a constant $C_\Omega$ such that $$\xi_i\bigl(xB^{(-t_i,t_i)}\bigr) \le C_\Omega e^{-2\eta t_i} \qquad(x\in\Omega,\ i\ge1).$$ Then $h_\xi(a(1))\ge\eta$.
  - Explanation: Exponential decay of two-sided tube masses yields a positive entropy lower bound when the weak limit remains a probability.
- **claim-dominated-probability-entropy** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-rig-dominated-entropy)): Let $\mu$ be an $A_4$-invariant probability on $X_4$ satisfying the compact ball bound in Equation <a href="#eq:ord-balls" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ord-balls">[eq:ord-balls]</a> for every compact set of centers. If $\xi$ is an $A_4$-invariant probability with $\xi\le c\mu$ for some finite $c$, then $$h_\xi(a(1))\ge \lambda_a/3.$$
  - Explanation: The compact ball estimate forces positive entropy for every invariant probability dominated by a finite multiple of the given measure.
- **claim-positive-entropy-components** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-rig-positive-components)): Let $\mu$ be an $A_4$-invariant probability on $X_4$ satisfying Equation <a href="#eq:ord-balls" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ord-balls">[eq:ord-balls]</a> on every compact set of centers. Almost every component in its $A_4$-ergodic decomposition has positive $a(1)$-entropy. In particular, this holds for every probability limit in Proposition <a href="#prop:ord-balls" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:ord-balls">11</a>.
  - Explanation: The entropy conclusion holds in almost every diagonal-ergodic component, rather than only for their average.
- **claim-positive-entropy-homogeneity** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-rig-ekl-classification)): For $n\ge3$, let $A_n$ be the group of positive diagonal matrices in $\mathrm{SL}_n(\mathbb R)$. Let $\nu$ be an $A_n$-invariant and $A_n$-ergodic probability on $\mathrm{SL}_n(\mathbb R)/\mathrm{SL}_n(\mathbb Z)$, with the left action. If $h_\nu(a_0(1))>0$ for some one-parameter subgroup $a_0:\mathbb R\to A_n$, then there are a closed connected subgroup $L\subset\mathrm{SL}_n(\mathbb R)$ containing $A_n$ and a point $x$ such that $Lx$ is closed and $\nu$ is the $L$-invariant probability on $Lx$.
  - Explanation: A positive-entropy diagonal-ergodic probability is the invariant probability on a closed homogeneous orbit.
- **claim-equal-block-rational-commutant** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-rig-equal-blocks)): Let $J$ be a closed connected subgroup of $\mathrm{SL}_4(\mathbb R)$ containing $A_4$, and let $\Lambda\in X_4$. Suppose that the stabilizer $\Gamma_\Lambda=\{j\in J:\Lambda j=\Lambda\}$ is a lattice in $J$. Then $J=J_{\mathcal I}$ for a partition $\mathcal I$ whose blocks all have the same size.* *More precisely, put $W_\mathbb Q=\mathbb Q\Lambda$ and $W_\mathbb R=W_\mathbb Q\otimes_\mathbb Q\mathbb R=\mathbb R^4$. There is a totally real number field $E\subset\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_\mathbb Q(W_\mathbb Q)$, of degree $s=\|\mathcal I\|$, whose scalar extension to $\mathbb R$ is exactly the commutant of $J$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_\mathbb R(W_\mathbb R)$. The coordinate blocks $\mathbb R^{I_v}$ are the real eigenspaces of $E$ on $W_\mathbb R$.
  - Explanation: Finite orbit volume forces equal coordinate-block sizes and identifies the block scalars with the real extension of a totally real commutant field.
- **claim-quartic-homogeneous-obstruction** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-rig-obstruction)): Let $\nu$ be an $A_4$-invariant, $A_4$-ergodic probability on $X_4$. Suppose that $h_\nu(a_0(1))>0$ for some one-parameter subgroup $a_0:\mathbb R\to A_4$. Then either $\nu=m_4$, or there exists $b\in\mathcal B$ such that $\nu(\mathcal E_b)=1$. In the latter case every lattice $\Lambda'$ in the homogeneous orbit carrying $\nu$ has a vector in $\bigwedge^2\Lambda'\cap V_b$ with both coordinates nonzero and with nonzero integer $Q$-value.
  - Explanation: A proper positive-entropy homogeneous component must carry an exterior vector in a fixed opposite-coordinate plane with nonzero integral quadratic value.
- **claim-rational-exterior-product-map** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-ext-products)): The expression in Equation <a href="#eq:ext-product-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ext-product-map">[eq:ext-product-map]</a> defines a rational quadratic map $\Psi:V\to F$. In the exterior basis of $L_V$ induced by $(\beta_i)$, write $w=\sum_{i<j}t_{ij}\beta_i\wedge\beta_j$. Then $$Q_V(w):=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{F/\mathbb Q}\Psi(w) =t_{12}t_{34}-t_{13}t_{24}+t_{14}t_{23}. \label{eq:ext-integral-form}$$ If $w\in V$ is nonzero, all six $w_{ij}$, and hence all three components of $\Psi(w)$, are nonzero. For every extension $k/\mathbb Q$, $a\in(K\otimes_\mathbb Qk)^\times$, $c\in k$, and $w\in V\otimes_\mathbb Qk$, $$\Psi\bigl(c(\bigwedge\nolimits^2 a)w\bigr) =c^2\mathop{\mathrm{N}}_{K\otimes k/k}(a)\Psi(w), \label{eq:ext-covariance}$$ where $\bigwedge^2a$ is the exterior square of multiplication by $a$.
  - Explanation: The signed opposite products descend to the cubic resolvent, have an integral trace form, and transform covariantly under scalar multiplication and the field action.
- **claim-planar-exterior-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-exterior-count)): There is an absolute $\tau>0$ with the following property. Fix $m\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$ and a bounded set $\Omega\subset\mathcal H_m$. For every primitive totally real quartic field $K$, every order $\mathcal O\subset K$, every ordering of its real embeddings, and every chosen system of fractional $R$-ideal representatives for the ordinary classes, let $M$ be defined by Equation <a href="#eq:ext-multiplicity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ext-multiplicity">[eq:ext-multiplicity]</a>. For all sufficiently large $X$, every rectangle $\mathcal R\subset\Omega$ whose two side lengths are at least $X^{-\tau}$ satisfies $$\sum_{\substack{z\in F,\ \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}z=m\\z\in\mathcal R}}M(z) \ll_{m,\Omega}\kappa\sqrt D\,\operatorname{area}(\mathcal R). \label{eq:ext-planar-count}$$ The implied constant and the threshold for $X$ are uniform in the field, the order, the embedding ordering, the ideal representative system, the position of the rectangle, and its side lengths and aspect ratio.
  - Explanation: Weighted exterior multiplicities in admissible trace-plane rectangles are bounded by area with the exact packet normalization and uniform conductor dependence.
- **claim-product-fiber-comparison** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-ext-comparison)): Let $w,w'\in V_k$ have all six embedding coordinates nonzero. Suppose that their coordinate ratios $r_{ij}=w'_{ij}/w_{ij}$ satisfy $$r_{12}r_{34}=r_{13}r_{24}=r_{14}r_{23}=s\in k^\times.$$ There exist $a\in K_k^\times$ and $c\in k^\times$ such that $$w'=c(\bigwedge\nolimits^2 a)w,\qquad c^2\mathop{\mathrm{N}}_{K_k/k}(a)=s.$$ The pair $(a,c)$ is unique up to $(a,c)\mapsto(da,d^{-2}c)$, $d\in k^\times$.
  - Explanation: Matching opposite-product ratios characterize the scalar and exterior field-multiplication relation between vectors, with only a scalar ambiguity.
- **claim-local-density-disintegration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ext-density)): The law in Equation <a href="#eq:ext-density-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ext-density-law">[eq:ext-density-law]</a> has a density, with a locally constant version on $F_p^\times$. For every $z\in F_p^\times$, only finitely many cosets are accepted, and their fractions satisfy the exact identity $$\sum_{\mathfrak c\in\mathscr G_p/\mathscr U_p} \alpha_p(\mathfrak c;z) = \|D\|_p^{-1/2} \frac{1-p^{-1}} {\displaystyle\prod_{\mathfrak p\mid p,\ \mathfrak p\subset R} (1-\mathop{\mathrm{N}}(\mathfrak p)^{-1})} f_p^*(z). \label{eq:ext-density-disintegration}$$ The sum and the density are both zero if $\mathscr F_p(z)$ is empty. The identity holds at every prime, including primes dividing $q$ and the small ramified primes.
  - Explanation: The local product law has a density, and exact fiber disintegration converts its value into the finite sum of accepted coset fractions at every prime.
- **claim-global-coset-multiplicity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ext-global-cosets)): Fix $z\in F^\times$. Choose a reference in each nonempty local fiber $\mathscr F_p(z)$. For any assignment $\mathfrak c_p\in\mathscr G_p/\mathscr U_p$, at most $32$ pairs $$I,\qquad w\in(\bigwedge^2_{\mathbb Z}I\setminus\{0\})/U^+,\qquad z(I,w)=z$$ occupy the assigned coset at every prime.
  - Explanation: A simultaneous assignment of local cosets admits only boundedly many global ideal-vector pairs modulo totally positive units.
- **claim-unramified-local-records** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ext-unramified-data)): Let $p\notin S$. A local fiber with an accepted coset has $z\in R_{F,p}$, and for $z\in R_{F,p}\cap F_p^\times$, $$\sum_{\mathfrak c\in\mathscr G_p/\mathscr U_p} \alpha_p(\mathfrak c;z)\le b_p(z). \label{eq:ext-unramified-cosets}$$ One has $b_p=1$ when $n_p=0$, and $0\le b_p\le(1+n_p)^3$.* *Give the affine lattice $$\{z\in R_{F,p}:\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{F_p/\mathbb Q_p}z=m\}$$ its Haar probability. With $t=p^{-1}$ and $f_{\mathfrak p}$ the residue degrees of the primes of $K$ above $p$, $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E_p b_p &=(1-t)(1-t^3) \prod_{\mathfrak p\mid p}(1-t^{f_{\mathfrak p}})^{-1} =1+\frac{a_K(p)-1}{p}+O(p^{-2}), \label{eq:ext-unramified-mean}\\ \mathbb P_p(n_p\ge k)&\le3p^{-k}\qquad(k\ge1). \label{eq:ext-unramified-tail} \end{aligned}$$ The error constant is absolute. In any integral affine coordinates on the trace lattice, $\{n_p\ge k\}$ is determined modulo $p^k$, and $b_p\mathbf1_{\{n_p=k\}}$ is determined modulo $p^{k+1}$ for every finite $k\ge0$.
  - Explanation: Frobenius valuation records bound the accepted unramified cosets and give their exact trace-plane mean, geometric valuation tail, and congruence dependence.
- **claim-global-local-multiplicity-majorant** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-ext-multiplicity-majorant)): For $z\in F$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}z=m\ne0$, a positive value of $M(z)$ requires $z$ to be integral outside $S$. For such integral $z$, $$M(z)\le32 \prod_{p\in S} \left( \|D\|_p^{-1/2} \frac{1-p^{-1}} {\displaystyle\prod_{\mathfrak p\mid p,\ \mathfrak p\subset R} (1-\mathop{\mathrm{N}}(\mathfrak p)^{-1})} f_p^*(z)\right) \prod_{p\notin S}b_p(z). \label{eq:ext-local-majorant}$$ All products in this assertion are finite at every contributing $z$.
  - Explanation: Global multiplicity is finite and bounded by a product of exact bad-prime densities and unramified valuation weights.
- **claim-self-adjoint-resolvent-module** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-ext-quadratic-structure)): The plane-scalar action descends to a rational action $\rho:F\to\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_{\mathbb Q}(V)$ for which $V$ has rank two over $F$. The form $Q_V$ in an exterior $\mathbb Z$-basis of $L_V$ is the three split pairs in Equation <a href="#eq:ext-integral-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ext-integral-form">[eq:ext-integral-form]</a>; in particular $B_V$ is unimodular on $L_V$. The action $\rho$ is self-adjoint for $B_V$, and for $\alpha\in F$, $$\begin{aligned} Q_\alpha(x):=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{F/\mathbb Q}(\alpha\Psi(x)) &=\tfrac12 B_V(\rho(\alpha)x,x), \label{eq:ext-polar-operator}\\ B_\alpha(x,y)&=B_V(\rho(\alpha)x,y), \notag\\ \det_{\mathbb Q}\rho(\alpha)&=\mathop{\mathrm{N}}_{F/\mathbb Q}(\alpha)^2,\qquad \Psi(\rho(\alpha)x)=\alpha^2\Psi(x). \label{eq:ext-rank-two} \end{aligned}$$ Here $B_\alpha$ is the polarization of $Q_\alpha$. The same assertions hold after base change to every $\mathbb Q_p$.
  - Explanation: The resolvent acts self-adjointly on the exterior space as a rank-two module, compatibly with the unimodular quadratic trace form and its local extensions.
- **claim-stabilizer-order-scale** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ext-order-scale)): There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\frac12 X^{1/2}\le \Delta_F=D_F[R_F:C_F]^2\le C X^{28}. \label{eq:ext-order-scale}$$ More precisely, with $d=\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(\mathcal O)=X^2$, $$d^2R_F\subset C_F\subset R_F,\qquad D_F\ll D^2.$$ In particular $C_{F,p}=R_{F,p}$ for $p\nmid Dq$.
  - Explanation: The stabilizer order has discriminant growing with the quartic order scale and bounded polynomially above, including when the resolvent field remains fixed.
- **claim-trace-dual-support** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ext-support)): One has $$=4,\qquad \Delta_F^\sharp:=\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(C_F^\sharp)=16\Delta_F\ll X^{28}, \label{eq:ext-sharp-discriminant}$$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}:(C_F^\sharp)^\vee\to\mathbb Z$ is onto. For $p\notin S$, $C_{F,p}^\sharp=(C_{F,p}^\sharp)^\vee=R_{F,p}$. For every prime $p$, the density $f_p^*$ on $F_p^\times$ is supported on $(C_{F,p}^\sharp)^\vee$. Consequently $$M(z)>0\ \Longrightarrow\ z\in(C_F^\sharp)^\vee.$$ The following stronger containment is used to average the majorant in Proposition <a href="#prop:ext-multiplicity-majorant" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:ext-multiplicity-majorant">24</a>: if $z\in F^\times$ is integral outside $S$ and $\prod_{p\in S}f_p^*(z)>0$, then $z\in(C_F^\sharp)^\vee$.* *Fix $m\ne0$ and a bounded $\Omega\subset\mathcal H_m$. Every $z\in(C_F^\sharp)^\vee\cap\Omega$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} &\Delta_F^\sharp z\in R_F,\qquad 1\le\|\mathop{\mathrm{N}}_{F/\mathbb Q}(\Delta_F^\sharp z)\|\ll_\Omega X^{84}, \label{eq:ext-norm-bound}\\ &\prod_{p\notin S}p^{n_p(z)} \le\|\mathop{\mathrm{N}}_{F/\mathbb Q}(\Delta_F^\sharp z)\|,\qquad \|z_b\|\gg_\Omega X^{-84}\quad(b\in\mathcal B). \label{eq:ext-depth-coordinate-bound} \end{aligned}$$ The constants are uniform in all arithmetic data and in the point of $\Omega$.
  - Explanation: The auxiliary trace dual contains all contributing products and controls their denominators, unramified depth, and nonzero real coordinate sizes. The opening index display is incomplete in the supplied statement.
- **claim-abstract-local-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fourier-density)): The measure $\mu_p$ has a density $f_p^*$ with respect to $dz$. It has a finite locally constant version at every $z=(z_\nu)\in E$ with $z_\nu\ne0$ for all $\nu$. This version vanishes outside $$(\mathcal C_L^\sharp)^\vee =\{z\in E:\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{E/\mathbb Q_p}(z\mathcal C_L^\sharp)\subset\mathbb Z_p\}.$$ In particular $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{E/\mathbb Q_p}(u\Psi_E(x))\in\mathbb Z_p$ on the domain of Equation <a href="#eq:fourier-density-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fourier-density-law">[eq:fourier-density-law]</a>.
  - Explanation: The abstract quadratic product law has a finite locally constant density away from vanishing components and is supported in the auxiliary trace dual.
- **claim-scalar-trace-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fourier-scalar-density)): Suppose that the integral scalar form is exactly $Q_1=x_1y_1-x_2y_2+x_3y_3$ as in Equation <a href="#eq:fourier-split-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fourier-split-form">[eq:fourier-split-form]</a>. Regard $a\in\mathbb Q_p$ as a scalar in $E$. In the quartic application this scalar form is $Q_1=Q_V$. Then, for every prime $p$, including $p=2$, $$\label{eq:fourier-scalar-coefficients} \gamma_p(a)= \begin{cases} 1,&a\in\mathbb Z_p,\\ p^{-3k},&v_p(a)=-k<0. \end{cases}$$ The trace of the law in Equation <a href="#eq:fourier-density-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fourier-density-law">[eq:fourier-density-law]</a> has density $d_p(t)\mathbf1_{\mathbb Z_p}(t)$ for additive measure giving $\mathbb Z_p$ mass $1$. This density is continuous on $\mathbb Z_p$ and satisfies $$\label{eq:fourier-scalar-density-bound} \|d_p(t)-1\|\le\frac1{p(p+1)}\qquad(t\in\mathbb Z_p).$$ In particular $5/6\le d_p(t)\le7/6$, and for every finite set of primes $S$ and every choice $t_p\in\mathbb Z_p$, $$\prod_{p\in S}d_p(t_p)\asymp1$$ with absolute upper and lower constants.
  - Explanation: The exact integral split form gives order-independent scalar Fourier coefficients and a trace density uniformly close to its constant main term.
- **claim-shell-scalar-shift** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-fourier-shift)): Put $\iota_p=1$ for $p\le5$ and $\iota_p=0$ for $p>5$. For every $A\in\mathscr S_{\mathfrak h_p}$ there is an $s\in\{0,1,2,3\}$ such that $\alpha=A+s\in E^\times$ and $$v_\nu(\alpha_\nu)=-h_\nu\quad(h_\nu>0),\qquad 0\le v_\nu(\alpha_\nu)\le e_\nu\iota_p\quad(h_\nu=0).$$ Writing $\beta=\alpha^{-1}$, one has $$\label{eq:fourier-shift-bounds} \beta\in p^{-\iota_p}\mathfrak h_p, \qquad p^{-3\iota_p}H_p \le \|\mathop{\mathrm{N}}_{E/\mathbb Q_p}\alpha\|_p\le H_p.$$ In particular the norm is exactly $H_p$ when $p>5$. If $$\label{eq:fourier-j-definition} j_p=j_p(A,s)=\min\{j\ge0:p^j\beta\in\mathcal C_L\},$$ then $j_p$ is finite and $j_p\le g_p+\iota_p$. Finally, for any generator $i\in E^\times$ of the ideal $\mathfrak h_p$, the element $x=i(A+s)$ is in $R_E$, is a unit of $R_E$ if $p>5$, and satisfies $x^{-1}\in p^{-\iota_p}R_E$.
  - Explanation: A bounded scalar shift makes every local factor invertible while preserving shell poles and controlling the inverse relative to the stabilizer order.
- **claim-quadratic-fourier-transformation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fourier-quadratic-transform)): Let $W_0$ be a finite-dimensional $\mathbb Q_p$-space with a nondegenerate symmetric form $B_0$, and let $L_0\subset W_0$ be a full $\mathbb Z_p$-lattice self-dual for $B_0$. Use the self-dual Haar measure, which gives $L_0$ mass $1$. For an invertible $B_0$-self-adjoint operator $P$, put $q_P(x)=B_0(Px,x)/2$. There is a phase $\omega(P)$ with $\|\omega(P)\|=1$ such that $$\label{eq:fourier-general-quadratic} \int_{L_0}\psi_p(q_P(x))\,dx =\omega(P)\|\det P\|_p^{-1/2} \int_{L_0}\psi_p(-q_{P^{-1}}(x))\,dx.$$ The normalized phase is invariant under linear isometry of the quadratic form: $\omega(T^*PT)=\omega(P)$ for every invertible $T$, where $T^*$ is the $B_0$-adjoint.* *For the local data above, $\alpha\in E^\times$, $\beta=\alpha^{-1}$ and $u\in\mathbb Z_p^\times$, this gives $$\label{eq:fourier-quadratic-transform} \int_L\psi_p(uQ_\alpha(x))\,dx =\omega_p(u,\alpha)\|\mathop{\mathrm{N}}_{E/\mathbb Q_p}\alpha\|_p^{-1} \int_L\psi_p(-u^{-1}Q_\beta(x))\,dx, \qquad \|\omega_p(u,\alpha)\|=1.$$ For every $r\in E^\times$ one has $\omega_p(u,\alpha r^2)=\omega_p(u,\alpha)$. All assertions hold at $p=2$.
  - Explanation: Quadratic Fourier transformation exchanges an invertible self-adjoint operator with its inverse, with a determinant factor and an isometry-invariant unit phase.
- **claim-intrinsic-fourier-shell-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fourier-shell-estimates)): Let $E$ be the cubic algebra above, let $W$ have rank two over $E$ with self-adjoint action for a nondegenerate symmetric form $B$, and let $L$ be self-dual for $B$, with $Q_1(L)\subset\mathbb Z_p$. Use the stabilizer order $\mathcal C_L=\{a\in E:\rho(a)L\subset L\}$ and $\Psi_E,\gamma_p$ from Equations <a href="#eq:fourier-polar-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fourier-polar-identity">[eq:fourier-polar-identity]</a> and <a href="#eq:fourier-gamma" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fourier-gamma">[eq:fourier-gamma]</a>. Fix an integral ideal $\mathfrak h_p=\prod_\nu\mathfrak q_\nu^{h_\nu}$, and use $H_p,g_p$ from Equation <a href="#eq:fourier-shell-indices" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fourier-shell-indices">[eq:fourier-shell-indices]</a>. The zero-extended function $\gamma_{p,\mathfrak h_p}$ and its inverse $K_{p,\mathfrak h_p}$ are Schwartz–Bruhat functions. Moreover, $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}K_{p,\mathfrak h_p}\subset(\mathcal C_L^\sharp)^\vee \subset\{z\in E:\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{E/\mathbb Q_p}z\in\mathbb Z_p\}.$$ For $A\in\mathscr S_{\mathfrak h_p}$ choose $s$ and $j_p=j_p(A,s)$ as in Lemma <a href="#lem:fourier-shift" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:fourier-shift">30</a>. Then $$\label{eq:fourier-shell-decay} \|\gamma_p(A)\|\le p^{3\iota_p}H_p^{-1}p^{-j_p/2}, \qquad \\|K_{p,\mathfrak h_p}\\|_\infty \le p^{3\iota_p}\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(R_E)\le p^{3\iota_p}.$$ Here $\iota_p=\mathbf1_{\{p\le5\}}$, so the first constant is $1$ at every $p>5$ and is at most $125$ at the three remaining primes.* *If $g_p>0$ or $p\le5$, then $$\label{eq:fourier-inverse-support} \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}K_{p,\mathfrak h_p} \subset p^{-4(g_p+1)}\mathfrak h_p\mathfrak d_E^{-1}.$$ If $g_p=0$ and $p>5$, then support instead requires, for each $\nu$, $$\label{eq:fourier-boundary-support} z_\nu\in \begin{cases} \mathfrak d_\nu^{-1},&h_\nu=0,\\ \mathfrak q_\nu^{h_\nu-1}\mathfrak d_\nu^{-1},&h_\nu>0. \end{cases}$$ For a $z$ satisfying these support conditions, define its set of boundary primes by $$\partial_{\mathfrak h_p}(z) =\{\mathfrak q_\nu:h_\nu>0, \ z_\nu\notin\mathfrak q_\nu^{h_\nu}\mathfrak d_\nu^{-1}\}.$$ In this case $$\label{eq:fourier-boundary-decay} \|K_{p,\mathfrak h_p}(z)\| \le 8\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(R_E) \prod_{\mathfrak q_\nu\in\partial_{\mathfrak h_p}(z)}q_\nu^{-1/2}.$$ Every constant is uniform in the lattice, the order $\mathcal C_L$, the shell, and the field factors and their ramification.
  - Explanation: Shell-restricted transforms have uniform decay and inverse-support bounds; shells contained in the stabilizer order admit sharper boundary decay.
- **claim-finite-pointwise-shell-inversion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fourier-inverse-expansion)): If $z=(z_\nu)\in E$ has $z_\nu\ne0$ for every $\nu$, then $$\label{eq:fourier-inverse-expansion} f_p^*(z)=\sum_{\mathfrak h_p}K_{p,\mathfrak h_p}(z),$$ where the sum is over integral $R_E$-ideals and only finitely many summands are nonzero.
  - Explanation: At outputs whose components do not vanish, the inverse-shell expansion is pointwise finite and equals the local density.
- **claim-additive-conductor-equidistribution** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-additive-equidistribution)): There are absolute constants $\tau,\theta>0$ with the following property. Let $K,\mathcal O,F$ be as in Section <a href="#sec:exterior" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exterior">5</a>, put $X=[\mathcal O_K:\mathcal O]\sqrt{\|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(K)\|}$, and take $S$ as in that section for the fixed nonzero integer $m$. In Equation <a href="#eq:add-box" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:add-box">[eq:add-box]</a>, choose any two real partition coordinates and scales $$X^{-\tau}\le r_i,r_j\le r_*$$ whose planar supports stay in the fixed bounded region above. Let $1\le l\le X^\theta$ be prime to every prime in $S$. For each $p\mid l$ choose any $z_p^0\in\mathcal T_{m,p}$, specifying a class modulo $p^{v_p(l)}$. Then $$\sum_{\substack{z\in F,\ \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}z=m\\ z\in R_{F,p}\ (p\notin S)\\ z-z_p^0\in p^{v_p(l)}R_{F,p}\ (p\mid l)}} \phi(z)\prod_{p\in S}f_p^*(z) = A_\phi\,l^{-2}\prod_{p\in S}d_p(m) \bigl(1+O(X^{-\theta})\bigr). \label{eq:add-equidistribution}$$ Here $d_p$ is the trace density of Lemma <a href="#lem:fourier-scalar-density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:fourier-scalar-density">29</a>. The error is uniform over all the fields, orders, ordered real embeddings, permitted box locations and scales, moduli $l$, and chosen residue classes. The implied constant and the lower threshold for $X$ depend only on the fixed real data listed above and on $m$.
  - Explanation: Products of conductor densities distribute uniformly on the real trace plane and in permitted residue classes, with a relative power-saving error.
- **claim-global-denominator-profile-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-add-profile-bounds)): For every profile one has $$G_0\le [R_F:C_F]\le H G_0. \label{eq:add-profile-indices}$$ Suppose that $z\in F$ is integral outside $S$, its real image lies in $\Omega_\infty$, and $\prod_{p\in S}K_{p,\mathfrak h_p}(z)\ne0$. Define $$T_\partial=T_\partial(\mathfrak h,z) =\prod_{\substack{p\in S\setminus\mathscr L_{\mathfrak h}\\ \mathfrak q\in\partial_{\mathfrak h_p}(z)}} \mathop{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak q ,$$ where an empty product is one. There is a constant depending only on $\Omega_\infty$ such that $$\begin{aligned} H&\ll D_FG_0^{24}T_\partial, &T_\partial&\le\left(\prod_{p\in S}p\right)^3, \label{eq:add-profile-norm}\\ \left\|\prod_{p\in S}K_{p,\mathfrak h_p}(z)\right\| &\ll 8^{\|S\|}T_\partial^{-1/2}. \label{eq:add-profile-inverse} \end{aligned}$$ All such $z$ lie in a fractional $R_F$-ideal $\mathfrak a_{\mathfrak h}$, independent of $z$, with $$\mathop{\mathrm{N}}(\mathfrak a_{\mathfrak h})^{-1} \le 30^{12}D_FG_0^{24}. \label{eq:add-profile-support}$$* *Set $C_3=371$. For all sufficiently large $X$, depending only on the fixed real data and $m$, a profile contributing at any such $z$ has $H\le X^{C_3}$. For these $X$ and every $z\in F$ integral outside $S$ with real image in $\Omega_\infty$, $$\prod_{p\in S}f_p^*(z) =\sum_{\substack{\mathfrak h=(\mathfrak h_p)_{p\in S}\\H\le X^{C_3}}} \prod_{p\in S}K_{p,\mathfrak h_p}(z). \label{eq:add-profile-expansion}$$ The number of profiles in this sum is $O_\eta(X^\eta)$ for every fixed $\eta>0$, uniformly in the arithmetic data.
  - Explanation: Inverse support and the nonzero field norm constrain denominator profiles, place supporting points in a fractional ideal, and produce a finite global density expansion.
- **claim-cubic-ideal-lattice-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-add-ideal-lattice)): Let $\mathfrak a$ be a fractional ideal of a totally real cubic field of discriminant $D_F$, and write $\mathop{\mathrm{N}}(\mathfrak a)=P^{-1}$ with $P>0$. Let $B_Y=\{x\in\mathbb R^3:\\|x\\|\le Y\}$. In embedding coordinates it has covolume $\sqrt{D_F}/P$, first Euclidean minimum $\gg P^{-1/3}$, and, for $Y>0$, $$\#\{A\in\mathfrak a:\\|A\\|\le Y\} \ll 1+YP^{1/3}+Y^2P^{2/3}+\frac{Y^3P}{\sqrt{D_F}} . \label{eq:add-lattice-count}$$ There is a $\mathbb Z$-basis $b_1,b_2,b_3$ of $\mathfrak a$ in which every $A=\sum n_i b_i$ with $\\|A\\|\le Y$ satisfies $$\|n_i\|\ll YP^{1/3}\quad(1\le i\le3).$$ All constants here are absolute.
  - Explanation: The ideal norm and field discriminant control covolume, the first minimum, lattice-point counts, and coordinates in a suitable integral basis.
- **claim-nonscalar-power-saving** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-add-nonscalar)): There are absolute $\tau,\theta,\epsilon_F>0$, with $\epsilon_F>2\tau+3\theta$, such that for $C_3=371$ and the data of Proposition <a href="#prop:additive-equidistribution" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:additive-equidistribution">34</a> with these $\tau,\theta$, $$\sum_{\substack{\mathfrak h\\H\le X^{C_3}}} \left\| \sum_{A\in F\setminus\mathbb Q} \widehat\phi(A)\widehat\eta_{\mathfrak h,l}(A) \right\| \ll X^{-\epsilon_F}. \label{eq:add-nonscalar}$$ The constants have the same uniformity as in that proposition.
  - Explanation: The total nonscalar Poisson contribution saves an absolute power, with enough margin to absorb shrinking boxes and the permitted residue moduli.
- **claim-nonnegative-residue-averages** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sieve-residue-function)): With $\phi$ and $V_\phi$ as in Equation <a href="#eq:sieve-measure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sieve-measure">[eq:sieve-measure]</a>, let $1\le l\le X^\theta$ be an integer prime to every prime of $S$. For every nonnegative function $W$ on the residue classes of the integral trace-$m$ coset modulo $l$, $$\label{eq:sieve-residue-function} \int W\,d\nu_\phi =V_\phi\mathbb E_l W+O\bigl(V_\phi X^{-\theta}\mathbb E_l W\bigr).$$ The implied constant is uniform in the fields, orders, embedding orderings, permitted box locations and scales, $l$, and $W$. It may depend on $m$, the fixed bounded region, and the fixed smooth box function.
  - Explanation: Nonnegative residue weights preserve their local mean in both the main term and the relative error.
- **claim-weighted-unramified-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-sieve-averaging)): For the measure $\nu_\phi$ in Equation <a href="#eq:sieve-measure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sieve-measure">[eq:sieve-measure]</a>, there is a fixed $\delta>0$ such that $$\label{eq:sieve-average} \int\mathcal W(z)\,d\nu_\phi(z) \ll V_\phi\prod_{\substack{p\le X^\delta\\p\notin S}}\mathbb E_p b_p.$$ The choice of $\delta$ and the implied constant are independent of the field, order, embedding ordering, and the permitted box location and scales. They may depend on $m$, the fixed bounded real region, and the fixed smooth box function.
  - Explanation: The complete unramified multiplicative weight averages to at most a truncated product of its local means, preserving factors below the constant main term.
- **claim-prescribed-depth-inactivity-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sieve-assignment)): Let $\mathcal A,\mathcal I$ be disjoint subsets of $\mathcal P_Y$, and assign an integer $j_p\ge1$ to each $p\in\mathcal A$. Suppose that $\prod_{p\in\mathcal A}p^{j_p}\le T_0^2$. Then $$\label{eq:sieve-assignment} \int \prod_{p\in\mathcal A} b_p\mathbf 1_{\{n_p=j_p\}} \prod_{p\in\mathcal I}\mathbf 1_{\{n_p=0\}}\, d\nu_\phi \ll V_\phi \prod_{p\in\mathcal A}\mathbb E_p \bigl(b_p\mathbf 1_{\{n_p=j_p\}}\bigr) \prod_{p\in\mathcal I}(1-\vartheta_p).$$ The constant is uniform in the two subsets and all their assigned depths.
  - Explanation: A Selberg upper-bound square controls simultaneous prescribed positive depths and inactivity at disjoint sets of small primes.
- **claim-exterior-packet-unfolding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-end-unfold)): For every primitive totally real quartic field $K$, order $\mathcal O$, and ordering of its real embeddings, let $d_U=[U:U^+]$. With the preceding choices of $b_1,m,L,r$, use the same fixed system of maximal-order ideal representatives in the packet model and in the definition of $M$. Then $$\label{eq:end-unfold} \int_{X_4}N_{m,L,r}(\Lambda)\,d\mu_{\mathcal O,\sigma}(\Lambda) =\frac{4}{d_U\sqrt D\,\kappa} \sum_{t\in\{m,-m\}} \sum_{\substack{z\in F\\\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}z=t}} M(z)\mathcal L_{L,r}(z).$$ The index satisfies $2\le d_U\le16$.
  - Explanation: Unfolding positive-unit orbits and all sign components expresses the packet exterior count through multiplicities on both signed trace slices and logarithmic fiber lengths.
- **claim-small-exterior-box-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-end-tube)): For every fixed $m\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$ and $L>1$, there are constants $r_0>0$ and $C_{m,L}>0$ with the following property. For every sequence of the fields, orders, and embedding orderings in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> with $X_i\to\infty$, and every fixed $0<r<r_0$, $$\label{eq:end-tube} \limsup_{i\to\infty} \int_{X_4}N_{m,L,r}(\Lambda)\, d\mu_{\mathcal O_i,\sigma_i}(\Lambda) \le C_{m,L}r^4.$$ The constants are independent of the sequence, of $r$, and of the choice of partition $b_1$.
  - Explanation: The planar count and summable logarithmic fibers bound the limiting packet average of exterior vectors concentrated near an obstruction plane.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ord-shells",
        "exact_quote": "With the preceding notation, $$\\label{eq:ord-shell-mass}\n \\sum_{\\boldsymbol l}\\frac{P(\\boldsymbol l)}{d(\\boldsymbol l)}\n       =\\frac{Z_q}{q}.$$ For every $\\eta>0$ there is a constant $C_\\eta$, independent of $K,\\mathcal O$, such that $$\\label{eq:ord-shell-moment}\n \\sum_{\\boldsymbol l}\n       P(\\boldsymbol l)d(\\boldsymbol l)^{-1+1/8}\n       \\le C_\\eta\\,\\frac{q^\\eta}{q}.$$ In particular there is an absolute constant $C$ for which $$\\label{eq:ord-shell-tail}\n \\sum_{d(\\boldsymbol l)>X^{1/8}}\n       \\frac{P(\\boldsymbol l)}{d(\\boldsymbol l)}\n       \\le \\frac Cq X^{-1/128}.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ord-residue",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $D_0>1$, $h_0>0$, $Y_0>1$, and $C\\ge1$ with the following properties for every primitive totally real quartic field $K$.*\n\n\n1.  *If $D\\ge D_0$, then $$\\label{eq:ord-residue-comparison}\n     C^{-1}\\kappa\\le h\\zeta_K(1+h)\\le C\\kappa\n           \\qquad(0<h\\le4/\\log D).$$ If $D<D_0$, the same inequalities hold for $0<h\\le h_0$.*\n\n\n2.  *One has $$\\label{eq:ord-residue-lower}\n     \\kappa^{-1}\\le C\\log(3D).$$*\n\n\n3.  *Whenever $Y\\ge\\max\\{Y_0,D^{1/4}\\}$, $$\\label{eq:ord-prime-sum}\n     \\exp\\left(\\sum_{p\\le Y}\\frac{a_K(p)}p\\right)\n           \\le C\\kappa\\log Y .$$*\n\n*The sum in Equation <a href=\"#eq:ord-prime-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ord-prime-sum\">[eq:ord-prime-sum]</a> is over rational primes.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ord-residue",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $D_0>1$, $h_0>0$, $Y_0>1$, and $C\\ge1$ with the following properties for every primitive totally real quartic field $K$.*\n\n\n1.  *If $D\\ge D_0$, then $$\\label{eq:ord-residue-comparison}\n     C^{-1}\\kappa\\le h\\zeta_K(1+h)\\le C\\kappa\n           \\qquad(0<h\\le4/\\log D).$$ If $D<D_0$, the same inequalities hold for $0<h\\le h_0$.*\n\n\n2.  *One has $$\\label{eq:ord-residue-lower}\n     \\kappa^{-1}\\le C\\log(3D).$$*\n\n\n3.  *Whenever $Y\\ge\\max\\{Y_0,D^{1/4}\\}$, $$\\label{eq:ord-prime-sum}\n     \\exp\\left(\\sum_{p\\le Y}\\frac{a_K(p)}p\\right)\n           \\le C\\kappa\\log Y .$$*\n\n*The sum in Equation <a href=\"#eq:ord-prime-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ord-prime-sum\">[eq:ord-prime-sum]</a> is over rational primes.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ord-shiu",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C,u_0$ such that, for every nonnegative multiplicative function $b$ with $b(n)\\le d_4(n)$, every real $u\\ge u_0$, and every $u^{1/2}\\le v\\le u$, $$\\label{eq:ord-shiu}\n \\sum_{u-v<n\\le u}b(n)\n \\le C\\frac v{\\log u}\n       \\exp\\left(\\sum_{p\\le u}\\frac{b(p)}p\\right).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-ord-weighted-intervals",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for each fixed $A\\ge1$ and $c>0$, there is a threshold $X_0(A,c)$ with the following property.         "source_id": "PM-015.2"

        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for every fixed $0<r<1$, $$\\label{eq:ord-cusp}\n \\limsup_{X\\longrightarrow\\infty}\n \\mu_*\\{\\Lambda:\\text{some }0\\ne v\\in\\Lambda\n                         \\text{ satisfies }\\|v\\|_\\infty<r\\}\n       \\le Cr^4 .$$ The limit superior ranges over all primitive totally real quartic fields, all their orders, and all orderings of their real embeddings.         "anchor": "stmt-thm-rig-tube-criterion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\xi_i$ and $\\xi$ be $a(\\mathbb R)$-invariant Borel probabilities on $X_4$, with $\\xi_i$ converging weak-\\* to $\\xi$. Assume that for every compact $\\Omega\\subset X_4$ there are an open identity neighborhood $B\\subset\\mathrm{SL}_4(\\mathbb R)$ and a constant $C_\\Omega$ such that $$\\xi_i\\bigl(xB^{(-t_i,t_i)}\\bigr)\n \\le C_\\Omega e^{-2\\eta t_i}\n \\qquad(x\\in\\Omega,\\ i\\ge1).$$ Then $h_\\xi(a(1))\\ge\\eta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-rig-tube-criterion",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rig-equal-blocks",
        "exact_quote": "Let $J$ be a closed connected subgroup of $\\mathrm{SL}_4(\\mathbb R)$ containing $A_4$, and let $\\Lambda\\in X_4$. Suppose that the stabilizer $\\Gamma_\\Lambda=\\{j\\in J:\\Lambda j=\\Lambda\\}$ is a lattice in $J$. Then $J=J_{\\mathcal I}$ for a partition $\\mathcal I$ whose blocks all have the same size.*\n\n*More precisely, put $W_\\mathbb Q=\\mathbb Q\\Lambda$ and $W_\\mathbb R=W_\\mathbb Q\\otimes_\\mathbb Q\\mathbb R=\\mathbb R^4$. There is a totally real number field $E\\subset\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_\\mathbb Q(W_\\mathbb Q)$, of degree $s=|\\mathcal I|$, whose scalar extension to $\\mathbb R$ is exactly the commutant of $J$ in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_\\mathbb R(W_\\mathbb R)$. The coordinate blocks $\\mathbb R^{I_v}$ are the real eigenspaces of $E$ on $W_\\mathbb R$.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-exterior-count",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute $\\tau>0$ with the following property. Fix $m\\in\\mathbb Z\\setminus\\{0\\}$ and a bounded set $\\Omega\\subset\\mathcal H_m$. For every primitive totally real quartic field $K$, every order $\\mathcal O\\subset K$, every ordering of its real embeddings, and every chosen system of fractional $R$-ideal representatives for the ordinary classes, let $M$ be defined by Equation <a href=\"#eq:ext-multiplicity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ext-multiplicity\">[eq:ext-multiplicity]</a>. For all sufficiently large $X$, every rectangle $\\mathcal R\\subset\\Omega$ whose two side lengths are at least $X^{-\\tau}$ satisfies $$\\sum_{\\substack{z\\in F,\\ \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}z=m\\\\z\\in\\mathcal R}}M(z)\n \\ll_{m,\\Omega}\\kappa\\sqrt D\\,\\operatorname{area}(\\mathcal R).\n \\label{eq:ext-planar-count}$$ The implied constant and the threshold for $X$ are uniform in the field, the order, the embedding ordering, the ideal representative system, the position of the rectangle, and its side lengths and aspect ratio.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-exterior-count",
          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute $\\tau>0$ with the following property. Fix $m\\in\\mathbb Z\\setminus\\{0\\}$ and a bounded set $\\Omega\\subset\\mathcal H_m$. For every primitive totally real quartic field $K$, every order $\\mathcal O\\subset K$, every ordering of its real embeddings, and every chosen system of fractional $R$-ideal representatives for the ordinary classes, let $M$ be defined by Equation <a href=\"#eq:ext-multiplicity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ext-multiplicity\">[eq:ext-multiplicity]</a>. For all sufficiently large $X$, every rectangle $\\mathcal R\\subset\\Omega$ whose two side lengths are at least $X^{-\\tau}$ satisfies $$\\sum_{\\substack{z\\in F,\\ \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}z=m\\\\z\\in\\mathcal R}}M(z)\n \\ll_{m,\\Omega}\\kappa\\sqrt D\\,\\operatorname{area}(\\mathcal R).\n \\label{eq:ext-planar-count}$$ The implied constant and the threshold for $X$ are uniform in the field, the order, the embedding ordering, the ideal representative system, the position of the rectangle, and its side lengths and aspect ratio.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is an absolute $\\tau>0$ with the following property. Fix $m\\in\\mathbb Z\\setminus\\{0\\}$ and a bounded set $\\Omega\\subset\\mathcal H_m$. For every primitive totally real quartic field $K$, every order $\\mathcal O\\subset K$, every ordering of its real embeddings, and every chosen system of fractional $R$-ideal representatives for the ordinary classes, let $M$ be defined by Equation <a href=\"#eq:ext-multiplicity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ext-multiplicity\">[eq:ext-multiplicity]</a>. For all sufficiently large $X$, every rectangle $\\mathcal R\\subset\\Omega$ whose two side lengths are at least $X^{-\\tau}$ satisfies $$\\sum_{\\substack{z\\in F,\\ \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}z=m\\\\z\\in\\mathcal R}}M(z)\n \\ll_{m,\\Omega}\\kappa\\sqrt D\\,\\operatorname{area}(\\mathcal R).\n \\label{eq:ext-planar-count}$$ The implied constant and the threshold for $X$ are uniform in the field, the order, the embedding ordering, the ideal representative system, the position of the rectangle, and its side lengths and aspect ratio.",
      "explanation": "Weighted exterior multiplicities in admissible trace-plane rectangles are bounded by area with the exact packet normalization and uniform conductor dependence.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ext-unramified-data",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p\\notin S$. A local fiber with an accepted coset has $z\\in R_{F,p}$, and for $z\\in R_{F,p}\\cap F_p^\\times$, $$\\sum_{\\mathfrak c\\in\\mathscr G_p/\\mathscr U_p}\n       \\alpha_p(\\mathfrak c;z)\\le b_p(z).\n \\label{eq:ext-unramified-cosets}$$ One has $b_p=1$ when $n_p=0$, and $0\\le b_p\\le(1+n_p)^3$.*\n\n\n\n*Give the affine lattice $$\\{z\\in R_{F,p}:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{F_p/\\mathbb Q_p}z=m\\}$$ its Haar probability. With $t=p^{-1}$ and $f_{\\mathfrak p}$ the residue degrees of the primes of $K$ above $p$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E_p b_p\n   &=(1-t)(1-t^3)\n       \\prod_{\\mathfrak p\\mid p}(1-t^{f_{\\mathfrak p}})^{-1}\n     =1+\\frac{a_K(p)-1}{p}+O(p^{-2}), \\label{eq:ext-unramified-mean}\\\\\n \\mathbb P_p(n_p\\ge k)&\\le3p^{-k}\\qquad(k\\ge1). \\label{eq:ext-unramified-tail}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The error constant is absolute. In any integral affine coordinates on the trace lattice, $\\{n_p\\ge k\\}$ is determined modulo $p^k$, and $b_p\\mathbf1_{\\{n_p=k\\}}$ is determined modulo $p^{k+1}$ for every finite $k\\ge0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ext-quadratic-structure",
        "exact_quote": "The plane-scalar action descends to a rational action $\\rho:F\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_{\\mathbb Q}(V)$ for which $V$ has rank two over $F$. The form $Q_V$ in an exterior $\\mathbb Z$-basis of $L_V$ is the three split pairs in Equation <a href=\"#eq:ext-integral-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ext-integral-form\">[eq:ext-integral-form]</a>; in particular $B_V$ is unimodular on $L_V$. The action $\\rho$ is self-adjoint for $B_V$, and for $\\alpha\\in F$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q_\\alpha(x):=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\alpha\\Psi(x))\n    &=\\tfrac12 B_V(\\rho(\\alpha)x,x), \\label{eq:ext-polar-operator}\\\\\n B_\\alpha(x,y)&=B_V(\\rho(\\alpha)x,y), \\notag\\\\\n \\det_{\\mathbb Q}\\rho(\\alpha)&=\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\alpha)^2,\\qquad\n \\Psi(\\rho(\\alpha)x)=\\alpha^2\\Psi(x). \\label{eq:ext-rank-two}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $B_\\alpha$ is the polarization of $Q_\\alpha$. The same assertions hold after base change to every $\\mathbb Q_p$.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ext-quadratic-structure",
          "exact_quote": "The plane-scalar action descends to a rational action $\\rho:F\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_{\\mathbb Q}(V)$ for which $V$ has rank two over $F$. The action $\\rho$ is self-adjoint for $B_V$, and for $\\alpha\\in F$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q_\\alpha(x):=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\alpha\\Psi(x))\n    &=\\tfrac12 B_V(\\rho(\\alpha)x,x), \\label{eq:ext-polar-operator}\\\\\n B_\\alpha(x,y)&=B_V(\\rho(\\alpha)x,y), \\notag\\\\\n \\det_{\\mathbb Q}\\rho(\\alpha)&=\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\alpha)^2,\\qquad\n \\Psi(\\rho(\\alpha)x)=\\alpha^2\\Psi(x). \\label{eq:ext-rank-two}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $B_\\alpha$ is the polarization of $Q_\\alpha$.       "exact_statement": "The plane-scalar action descends to a rational action $\\rho:F\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_{\\mathbb Q}(V)$ for which $V$ has rank two over $F$. The form $Q_V$ in an exterior $\\mathbb Z$-basis of $L_V$ is the three split pairs in Equation <a href=\"#eq:ext-integral-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ext-integral-form\">[eq:ext-integral-form]</a>; in particular $B_V$ is unimodular on $L_V$. The action $\\rho$ is self-adjoint for $B_V$, and for $\\alpha\\in F$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q_\\alpha(x):=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\alpha\\Psi(x))\n    &=\\tfrac12 B_V(\\rho(\\alpha)x,x), \\label{eq:ext-polar-operator}\\\\\n B_\\alpha(x,y)&=B_V(\\rho(\\alpha)x,y), \\notag\\\\\n \\det_{\\mathbb Q}\\rho(\\alpha)&=\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\alpha)^2,\\qquad\n \\Psi(\\rho(\\alpha)x)=\\alpha^2\\Psi(x). \\label{eq:ext-rank-two}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $B_\\alpha$ is the polarization of $Q_\\alpha$. The same assertions hold after base change to every $\\mathbb Q_p$.",
      "explanation": "The resolvent acts self-adjointly on the exterior space as a rank-two module, compatibly with the unimodular quadratic trace form and its local extensions.",

      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ext-quadratic-structure",
          "exact_quote": "The plane-scalar action descends to a rational action $\\rho:F\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_{\\mathbb Q}(V)$ for which $V$ has rank two over $F$. The form $Q_V$ in an exterior $\\mathbb Z$-basis of $L_V$ is the three split pairs in Equation <a href=\"#eq:ext-integral-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ext-integral-form\">[eq:ext-integral-form]</a>; in particular $B_V$ is unimodular on $L_V$. The action $\\rho$ is self-adjoint for $B_V$, and for $\\alpha\\in F$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q_\\alpha(x):=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\alpha\\Psi(x))\n    &=\\tfrac12 B_V(\\rho(\\alpha)x,x), \\label{eq:ext-polar-operator}\\\\\n B_\\alpha(x,y)&=B_V(\\rho(\\alpha)x,y), \\notag\\\\\n \\det_{\\mathbb Q}\\rho(\\alpha)&=\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\alpha)^2,\\qquad\n \\Psi(\\rho(\\alpha)x)=\\alpha^2\\Psi(x). \\label{eq:ext-rank-two}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $B_\\alpha$ is the polarization of $Q_\\alpha$. The same assertions hold after base change to every $\\mathbb Q_p$.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ext-order-scale",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\\frac12 X^{1/2}\\le\n \\Delta_F=D_F[R_F:C_F]^2\\le C X^{28}.\n \\label{eq:ext-order-scale}$$ More precisely, with $d=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Disc}}(\\mathcal O)=X^2$, $$d^2R_F\\subset C_F\\subset R_F,\\qquad D_F\\ll D^2.$$ In particular $C_{F,p}=R_{F,p}$ for $p\\nmid Dq$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ext-support",
        "exact_quote": "One has $$=4,\\qquad\n \\Delta_F^\\sharp:=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Disc}}(C_F^\\sharp)=16\\Delta_F\\ll X^{28},\n \\label{eq:ext-sharp-discriminant}$$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}:(C_F^\\sharp)^\\vee\\to\\mathbb Z$ is onto. Every $z\\in(C_F^\\sharp)^\\vee\\cap\\Omega$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\Delta_F^\\sharp z\\in R_F,\\qquad\n 1\\le|\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\Delta_F^\\sharp z)|\\ll_\\Omega X^{84},\n       \\label{eq:ext-norm-bound}\\\\\n &\\prod_{p\\notin S}p^{n_p(z)}\n       \\le|\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\Delta_F^\\sharp z)|,\\qquad\n |z_b|\\gg_\\Omega X^{-84}\\quad(b\\in\\mathcal B).\n \\label{eq:ext-depth-coordinate-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constants are uniform in all arithmetic data and in the point of $\\Omega$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-scalar-density",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that the integral scalar form is exactly $Q_1=x_1y_1-x_2y_2+x_3y_3$ as in Equation <a href=\"#eq:fourier-split-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-split-form\">[eq:fourier-split-form]</a>. Regard $a\\in\\mathbb Q_p$ as a scalar in $E$. In the quartic application this scalar form is $Q_1=Q_V$. Then, for every prime $p$, including $p=2$, $$\\label{eq:fourier-scalar-coefficients}\n \\gamma_p(a)=\n \\begin{cases}\n 1,&a\\in\\mathbb Z_p,\\\\\n p^{-3k},&v_p(a)=-k<0.\n \\end{cases}$$ The trace of the law in Equation <a href=\"#eq:fourier-density-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-density-law\">[eq:fourier-density-law]</a> has density $d_p(t)\\mathbf1_{\\mathbb Z_p}(t)$ for additive measure giving $\\mathbb Z_p$ mass $1$. This density is continuous on $\\mathbb Z_p$ and satisfies $$\\label{eq:fourier-scalar-density-bound}\n |d_p(t)-1|\\le\\frac1{p(p+1)}\\qquad(t\\in\\mathbb Z_p).$$ In particular $5/6\\le d_p(t)\\le7/6$, and for every finite set of primes $S$ and every choice $t_p\\in\\mathbb Z_p$, $$\\prod_{p\\in S}d_p(t_p)\\asymp1$$ with absolute upper and lower constants.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },

Regard $a\\in\\mathbb Q_p$ as a scalar in $E$. In the quartic application this scalar form is $Q_1=Q_V$. Then, for every prime $p$, including $p=2$, $$\\label{eq:fourier-scalar-coefficients}\n \\gamma_p(a)=\n \\begin{cases}\n 1,&a\\in\\mathbb Z_p,\\\\\n p^{-3k},&v_p(a)=-k<0.\n \\end{cases}$$ The trace of the law in Equation <a href=\"#eq:fourier-density-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-density-law\">[eq:fourier-density-law]</a> has density $d_p(t)\\mathbf1_{\\mathbb Z_p}(t)$ for additive measure giving $\\mathbb Z_p$ mass $1$. This density is continuous on $\\mathbb Z_p$ and satisfies $$\\label{eq:fourier-scalar-density-bound}\n |d_p(t)-1|\\le\\frac1{p(p+1)}\\qquad(t\\in\\mathbb Z_p).$$ In particular $5/6\\le d_p(t)\\le7/6$, and for every finite set of primes $S$ and every choice $t_p\\in\\mathbb Z_p$, $$\\prod_{p\\in S}d_p(t_p)\\asymp1$$ with absolute upper and lower constants.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-shift",
        "exact_quote": "Put $\\iota_p=1$ for $p\\le5$ and $\\iota_p=0$ for $p>5$. For every $A\\in\\mathscr S_{\\mathfrak h_p}$ there is an $s\\in\\{0,1,2,3\\}$ such that $\\alpha=A+s\\in E^\\times$ and $$v_\\nu(\\alpha_\\nu)=-h_\\nu\\quad(h_\\nu>0),\\qquad\n 0\\le v_\\nu(\\alpha_\\nu)\\le e_\\nu\\iota_p\\quad(h_\\nu=0).$$ Writing $\\beta=\\alpha^{-1}$, one has $$\\label{eq:fourier-shift-bounds}\n \\beta\\in p^{-\\iota_p}\\mathfrak h_p,\n \\qquad p^{-3\\iota_p}H_p\n       \\le |\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{E/\\mathbb Q_p}\\alpha|_p\\le H_p.$$ In particular the norm is exactly $H_p$ when $p>5$. If $$\\label{eq:fourier-j-definition}\n j_p=j_p(A,s)=\\min\\{j\\ge0:p^j\\beta\\in\\mathcal C_L\\},$$ then $j_p$ is finite and $j_p\\le g_p+\\iota_p$. Finally, for any generator $i\\in E^\\times$ of the ideal $\\mathfrak h_p$, the element $x=i(A+s)$ is in $R_E$, is a unit of $R_E$ if $p>5$, and satisfies $x^{-1}\\in p^{-\\iota_p}R_E$.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },

If $$\\label{eq:fourier-j-definition}\n j_p=j_p(A,s)=\\min\\{j\\ge0:p^j\\beta\\in\\mathcal C_L\\},$$ then $j_p$ is finite and $j_p\\le g_p+\\iota_p$. Finally, for any generator $i\\in E^\\times$ of the ideal $\\mathfrak h_p$, the element $x=i(A+s)$ is in $R_E$, is a unit of $R_E$ if $p>5$, and satisfies $x^{-1}\\in p^{-\\iota_p}R_E$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-quadratic-transform",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W_0$ be a finite-dimensional $\\mathbb Q_p$-space with a nondegenerate symmetric form $B_0$, and let $L_0\\subset W_0$ be a full $\\mathbb Z_p$-lattice self-dual for $B_0$. Use the self-dual Haar measure, which gives $L_0$ mass $1$. For an invertible $B_0$-self-adjoint operator $P$, put $q_P(x)=B_0(Px,x)/2$. There is a phase $\\omega(P)$ with $|\\omega(P)|=1$ such that $$\\label{eq:fourier-general-quadratic}\n \\int_{L_0}\\psi_p(q_P(x))\\,dx\n =\\omega(P)|\\det P|_p^{-1/2}\n       \\int_{L_0}\\psi_p(-q_{P^{-1}}(x))\\,dx.$$ The normalized phase is invariant under linear isometry of the quadratic form: $\\omega(T^*PT)=\\omega(P)$ for every invertible $T$, where $T^*$ is the $B_0$-adjoint.*\n\n\n*For the local data above, $\\alpha\\in E^\\times$, $\\beta=\\alpha^{-1}$ and $u\\in\\mathbb Z_p^\\times$, this gives $$\\label{eq:fourier-quadratic-transform}\n \\int_L\\psi_p(uQ_\\alpha(x))\\,dx\n =\\omega_p(u,\\alpha)|\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{E/\\mathbb Q_p}\\alpha|_p^{-1}\n       \\int_L\\psi_p(-u^{-1}Q_\\beta(x))\\,dx,\n \\qquad |\\omega_p(u,\\alpha)|=1.$$ For every $r\\in E^\\times$ one has $\\omega_p(u,\\alpha r^2)=\\omega_p(u,\\alpha)$. All assertions hold at $p=2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-shell-estimates",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be the cubic algebra above, let $W$ have rank two over $E$ with self-adjoint action for a nondegenerate symmetric form $B$, and let $L$ be self-dual for $B$, with $Q_1(L)\\subset\\mathbb Z_p$. Use the stabilizer order $\\mathcal C_L=\\{a\\in E:\\rho(a)L\\subset L\\}$ and $\\Psi_E,\\gamma_p$ from Equations <a href=\"#eq:fourier-polar-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-polar-identity\">[eq:fourier-polar-identity]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:fourier-gamma\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-gamma\">[eq:fourier-gamma]</a>. Fix an integral ideal $\\mathfrak h_p=\\prod_\\nu\\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu}$, and use $H_p,g_p$ from Equation <a href=\"#eq:fourier-shell-indices\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-shell-indices\">[eq:fourier-shell-indices]</a>. The zero-extended function $\\gamma_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}$ and its inverse $K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}$ are Schwartz–Bruhat functions. Moreover, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}\\subset(\\mathcal C_L^\\sharp)^\\vee\n \\subset\\{z\\in E:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{E/\\mathbb Q_p}z\\in\\mathbb Z_p\\}.$$ For $A\\in\\mathscr S_{\\mathfrak h_p}$ choose $s$ and $j_p=j_p(A,s)$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:fourier-shift\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:fourier-shift\">30</a>. Then $$\\label{eq:fourier-shell-decay}\n |\\gamma_p(A)|\\le p^{3\\iota_p}H_p^{-1}p^{-j_p/2},\n \\qquad\n \\|K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}\\|_\\infty\n       \\le p^{3\\iota_p}\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(R_E)\\le p^{3\\iota_p}.$$ Here $\\iota_p=\\mathbf1_{\\{p\\le5\\}}$, so the first constant is $1$ at every $p>5$ and is at most $125$ at the three remaining primes.*\n\n\n\n\n*If $g_p>0$ or $p\\le5$, then $$\\label{eq:fourier-inverse-support}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}\n       \\subset p^{-4(g_p+1)}\\mathfrak h_p\\mathfrak d_E^{-1}.$$ If $g_p=0$ and $p>5$, then support instead requires, for each $\\nu$, $$\\label{eq:fourier-boundary-support}\n z_\\nu\\in\n \\begin{cases}\n \\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1},&h_\\nu=0,\\\\\n \\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu-1}\\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1},&h_\\nu>0.\n \\end{cases}$$ For a $z$ satisfying these support conditions, define its set of boundary primes by $$\\partial_{\\mathfrak h_p}(z)\n =\\{\\mathfrak q_\\nu:h_\\nu>0,\n       \\ z_\\nu\\notin\\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu}\\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1}\\}.$$ In this case $$\\label{eq:fourier-boundary-decay}\n |K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z)|\n \\le 8\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(R_E)\n       \\prod_{\\mathfrak q_\\nu\\in\\partial_{\\mathfrak h_p}(z)}q_\\nu^{-1/2}.$$ Every constant is uniform in the lattice, the order $\\mathcal C_L$, the shell, and the field factors and their ramification.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-shell-estimates",
          "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be the cubic algebra above, let $W$ have rank two over $E$ with self-adjoint action for a nondegenerate symmetric form $B$, and let $L$ be self-dual for $B$, with $Q_1(L)\\subset\\mathbb Z_p$. Use the stabilizer order $\\mathcal C_L=\\{a\\in E:\\rho(a)L\\subset L\\}$ and $\\Psi_E,\\gamma_p$ from Equations <a href=\"#eq:fourier-polar-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-polar-identity\">[eq:fourier-polar-identity]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:fourier-gamma\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-gamma\">[eq:fourier-gamma]</a>. Fix an integral ideal $\\mathfrak h_p=\\prod_\\nu\\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu}$, and use $H_p,g_p$ from Equation <a href=\"#eq:fourier-shell-indices\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-shell-indices\">[eq:fourier-shell-indices]</a>. Then $$\\label{eq:fourier-shell-decay}\n |\\gamma_p(A)|\\le p^{3\\iota_p}H_p^{-1}p^{-j_p/2},\n \\qquad\n \\|K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}\\|_\\infty\n       \\le p^{3\\iota_p}\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(R_E)\\le p^{3\\iota_p}.$$ Here $\\iota_p=\\mathbf1_{\\{p\\le5\\}}$, so the first constant is $1$ at every $p>5$ and is at most $125$ at the three remaining primes.*\n\n\n\n\n*If $g_p>0$ or $p\\le5$, then $$\\label{eq:fourier-inverse-support}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}\n       \\subset p^{-4(g_p+1)}\\mathfrak h_p\\mathfrak d_E^{-1}.$$ If $g_p=0$ and $p>5$, then support instead requires, for each $\\nu$, $$\\label{eq:fourier-boundary-support}\n z_\\nu\\in\n \\begin{cases}\n \\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1},&h_\\nu=0,\\\\\n \\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu-1}\\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1},&h_\\nu>0.\n \\end{cases}$$ For a $z$ satisfying these support conditions, define its set of boundary primes by $$\\partial_{\\mathfrak h_p}(z)\n =\\{\\mathfrak q_\\nu:h_\\nu>0,\n       \\ z_\\nu\\notin\\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu}\\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1}\\}.$$ In this case $$\\label{eq:fourier-boundary-decay}\n |K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z)|\n \\le 8\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(R_E)\n       \\prod_{\\mathfrak q_\\nu\\in\\partial_{\\mathfrak h_p}(z)}q_\\nu^{-1/2}.$$ Every constant is uniform in the lattice, the order $\\mathcal C_L$, the shell, and the field factors and their ramification.",

        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $E$ be the cubic algebra above, let $W$ have rank two over $E$ with self-adjoint action for a nondegenerate symmetric form $B$, and let $L$ be self-dual for $B$, with $Q_1(L)\\subset\\mathbb Z_p$. Use the stabilizer order $\\mathcal C_L=\\{a\\in E:\\rho(a)L\\subset L\\}$ and $\\Psi_E,\\gamma_p$ from Equations <a href=\"#eq:fourier-polar-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-polar-identity\">[eq:fourier-polar-identity]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:fourier-gamma\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-gamma\">[eq:fourier-gamma]</a>. Then $$\\label{eq:fourier-shell-decay}\n |\\gamma_p(A)|\\le p^{3\\iota_p}H_p^{-1}p^{-j_p/2},\n \\qquad\n \\|K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}\\|_\\infty\n       \\le p^{3\\iota_p}\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(R_E)\\le p^{3\\iota_p}.$$ Here $\\iota_p=\\mathbf1_{\\{p\\le5\\}}$, so the first constant is $1$ at every $p>5$ and is at most $125$ at the three remaining primes.*\n\n\n\n\n*If $g_p>0$ or $p\\le5$, then $$\\label{eq:fourier-inverse-support}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}\n       \\subset p^{-4(g_p+1)}\\mathfrak h_p\\mathfrak d_E^{-1}.$$ If $g_p=0$ and $p>5$, then support instead requires, for each $\\nu$, $$\\label{eq:fourier-boundary-support}\n z_\\nu\\in\n \\begin{cases}\n \\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1},&h_\\nu=0,\\\\\n \\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu-1}\\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1},&h_\\nu>0.\n \\end{cases}$$ For a $z$ satisfying these support conditions, define its set of boundary primes by $$\\partial_{\\mathfrak h_p}(z)\n =\\{\\mathfrak q_\\nu:h_\\nu>0,\n       \\ z_\\nu\\notin\\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu}\\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1}\\}.$$ In this case $$\\label{eq:fourier-boundary-decay}\n |K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z)|\n \\le 8\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(R_E)\n       \\prod_{\\mathfrak q_\\nu\\in\\partial_{\\mathfrak h_p}(z)}q_\\nu^{-1/2}.$$ Every constant is uniform in the lattice, the order $\\mathcal C_L$, the shell, and the field factors and their ramification.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-inverse-expansion",
        "exact_quote": "If $z=(z_\\nu)\\in E$ has $z_\\nu\\ne0$ for every $\\nu$, then $$\\label{eq:fourier-inverse-expansion}\n f_p^*(z)=\\sum_{\\mathfrak h_p}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z),$$ where the sum is over integral $R_E$-ideals and only finitely many summands are nonzero.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-additive-equidistribution",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $\\tau,\\theta>0$ with the following property. Let $K,\\mathcal O,F$ be as in Section <a href=\"#sec:exterior\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:exterior\">5</a>, put $X=[\\mathcal O_K:\\mathcal O]\\sqrt{|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Disc}}(K)|}$, and take $S$ as in that section for the fixed nonzero integer $m$. In Equation <a href=\"#eq:add-box\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:add-box\">[eq:add-box]</a>, choose any two real partition coordinates and scales $$X^{-\\tau}\\le r_i,r_j\\le r_*$$ whose planar supports stay in the fixed bounded region above. Let $1\\le l\\le X^\\theta$ be prime to every prime in $S$. For each $p\\mid l$ choose any $z_p^0\\in\\mathcal T_{m,p}$, specifying a class modulo $p^{v_p(l)}$. Then $$\\sum_{\\substack{z\\in F,\\ \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}z=m\\\\\n                  z\\in R_{F,p}\\ (p\\notin S)\\\\\n                  z-z_p^0\\in p^{v_p(l)}R_{F,p}\\ (p\\mid l)}}\n       \\phi(z)\\prod_{p\\in S}f_p^*(z)\n =\n A_\\phi\\,l^{-2}\\prod_{p\\in S}d_p(m)\n       \\bigl(1+O(X^{-\\theta})\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:add-equidistribution}$$ Here $d_p$ is the trace density of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:fourier-scalar-density\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:fourier-scalar-density\">29</a>. The error is uniform over all the fields, orders, ordered real embeddings, permitted box locations and scales, moduli $l$, and chosen residue classes. The implied constant and the lower threshold for $X$ depend only on the fixed real data listed above and on $m$.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-additive-equidistribution",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $\\tau,\\theta>0$ with the following property. In Equation <a href=\"#eq:add-box\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:add-box\">[eq:add-box]</a>, choose any two real partition coordinates and scales $$X^{-\\tau}\\le r_i,r_j\\le r_*$$ whose planar supports stay in the fixed bounded region above.       "exact_statement": "There are absolute constants $\\tau,\\theta>0$ with the following property. Let $K,\\mathcal O,F$ be as in Section <a href=\"#sec:exterior\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:exterior\">5</a>, put $X=[\\mathcal O_K:\\mathcal O]\\sqrt{|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Disc}}(K)|}$, and take $S$ as in that section for the fixed nonzero integer $m$. In Equation <a href=\"#eq:add-box\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:add-box\">[eq:add-box]</a>, choose any two real partition coordinates and scales $$X^{-\\tau}\\le r_i,r_j\\le r_*$$ whose planar supports stay in the fixed bounded region above. Let $1\\le l\\le X^\\theta$ be prime to every prime in $S$. For each $p\\mid l$ choose any $z_p^0\\in\\mathcal T_{m,p}$, specifying a class modulo $p^{v_p(l)}$. Then $$\\sum_{\\substack{z\\in F,\\ \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}z=m\\\\\n                  z\\in R_{F,p}\\ (p\\notin S)\\\\\n                  z-z_p^0\\in p^{v_p(l)}R_{F,p}\\ (p\\mid l)}}\n       \\phi(z)\\prod_{p\\in S}f_p^*(z)\n =\n A_\\phi\\,l^{-2}\\prod_{p\\in S}d_p(m)\n       \\bigl(1+O(X^{-\\theta})\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:add-equidistribution}$$ Here $d_p$ is the trace density of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:fourier-scalar-density\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:fourier-scalar-density\">29</a>. The error is uniform over all the fields, orders, ordered real embeddings, permitted box locations and scales, moduli $l$, and chosen residue classes. The implied constant and the lower threshold for $X$ depend only on the fixed real data listed above and on $m$.",
      "explanation": "Products of conductor densities distribute uniformly on the real trace plane and in permitted residue classes, with a relative power-saving error.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-add-profile-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "For every profile one has $$G_0\\le [R_F:C_F]\\le H G_0.\n \\label{eq:add-profile-indices}$$ Suppose that $z\\in F$ is integral outside $S$, its real image lies in $\\Omega_\\infty$, and $\\prod_{p\\in S}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z)\\ne0$. Define $$T_\\partial=T_\\partial(\\mathfrak h,z)\n   =\\prod_{\\substack{p\\in S\\setminus\\mathscr L_{\\mathfrak h}\\\\\n                     \\mathfrak q\\in\\partial_{\\mathfrak h_p}(z)}}\n       \\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}\\mathfrak q ,$$ where an empty product is one. There is a constant depending only on $\\Omega_\\infty$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n H&\\ll D_FG_0^{24}T_\\partial,\n &T_\\partial&\\le\\left(\\prod_{p\\in S}p\\right)^3,\n \\label{eq:add-profile-norm}\\\\\n \\left|\\prod_{p\\in S}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z)\\right|\n   &\\ll 8^{|S|}T_\\partial^{-1/2}.\n \\label{eq:add-profile-inverse}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All such $z$ lie in a fractional $R_F$-ideal $\\mathfrak a_{\\mathfrak h}$, independent of $z$, with $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}(\\mathfrak a_{\\mathfrak h})^{-1}\n       \\le 30^{12}D_FG_0^{24}.\n \\label{eq:add-profile-support}$$*\n\n\n*Set $C_3=371$. For all sufficiently large $X$, depending only on the fixed real data and $m$, a profile contributing at any such $z$ has $H\\le X^{C_3}$. For these $X$ and every $z\\in F$ integral outside $S$ with real image in $\\Omega_\\infty$, $$\\prod_{p\\in S}f_p^*(z)\n  =\\sum_{\\substack{\\mathfrak h=(\\mathfrak h_p)_{p\\in S}\\\\H\\le X^{C_3}}}\n        \\prod_{p\\in S}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z).\n \\label{eq:add-profile-expansion}$$ The number of profiles in this sum is $O_\\eta(X^\\eta)$ for every fixed $\\eta>0$, uniformly in the arithmetic data.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-add-ideal-lattice",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathfrak a$ be a fractional ideal of a totally real cubic field of discriminant $D_F$, and write $\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}(\\mathfrak a)=P^{-1}$ with $P>0$. Let $B_Y=\\{x\\in\\mathbb R^3:\\|x\\|\\le Y\\}$. In embedding coordinates it has covolume $\\sqrt{D_F}/P$, first Euclidean minimum $\\gg P^{-1/3}$, and, for $Y>0$, $$\\#\\{A\\in\\mathfrak a:\\|A\\|\\le Y\\}\n  \\ll 1+YP^{1/3}+Y^2P^{2/3}+\\frac{Y^3P}{\\sqrt{D_F}} .\n \\label{eq:add-lattice-count}$$ There is a $\\mathbb Z$-basis $b_1,b_2,b_3$ of $\\mathfrak a$ in which every $A=\\sum n_i b_i$ with $\\|A\\|\\le Y$ satisfies $$|n_i|\\ll YP^{1/3}\\quad(1\\le i\\le3).$$ All constants here are absolute.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-add-nonscalar",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute $\\tau,\\theta,\\epsilon_F>0$, with $\\epsilon_F>2\\tau+3\\theta$, such that for $C_3=371$ and the data of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:additive-equidistribution\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:additive-equidistribution\">34</a> with these $\\tau,\\theta$, $$\\sum_{\\substack{\\mathfrak h\\\\H\\le X^{C_3}}}\n   \\left|\n    \\sum_{A\\in F\\setminus\\mathbb Q}\n       \\widehat\\phi(A)\\widehat\\eta_{\\mathfrak h,l}(A)\n   \\right|\n     \\ll X^{-\\epsilon_F}.\n \\label{eq:add-nonscalar}$$ The constants have the same uniformity as in that proposition.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "With $\\phi$ and $V_\\phi$ as in Equation <a href=\"#eq:sieve-measure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:sieve-measure\">[eq:sieve-measure]</a>, let $1\\le l\\le X^\\theta$ be an integer prime to every prime of $S$. For every nonnegative function $W$ on the residue classes of the integral trace-$m$ coset modulo $l$, $$\\label{eq:sieve-residue-function}\n \\int W\\,d\\nu_\\phi\n   =V_\\phi\\mathbb E_l W+O\\bigl(V_\\phi X^{-\\theta}\\mathbb E_l W\\bigr).$$ The implied constant is uniform in the fields, orders, embedding orderings, permitted box locations and scales, $l$, and $W$. It may depend on $m$, the fixed bounded region, and the fixed smooth box function.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sieve-assignment",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal A,\\mathcal I$ be disjoint subsets of $\\mathcal P_Y$, and assign an integer $j_p\\ge1$ to each $p\\in\\mathcal A$. Suppose that $\\prod_{p\\in\\mathcal A}p^{j_p}\\le T_0^2$. Then $$\\label{eq:sieve-assignment}\n \\int\n   \\prod_{p\\in\\mathcal A}\n      b_p\\mathbf 1_{\\{n_p=j_p\\}}\n   \\prod_{p\\in\\mathcal I}\\mathbf 1_{\\{n_p=0\\}}\\,\n        d\\nu_\\phi\n \\ll V_\\phi\n   \\prod_{p\\in\\mathcal A}\\mathbb E_p\n      \\bigl(b_p\\mathbf 1_{\\{n_p=j_p\\}}\\bigr)\n   \\prod_{p\\in\\mathcal I}(1-\\vartheta_p).$$ The constant is uniform in the two subsets and all their assigned depths.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.2"
      },
