<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Primitive quartic field** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A quartic field $K$ is *primitive* if it has no field strictly between $\mathbb Q$ and $K$.
- **Order** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here an order is a full-rank subring containing $1$.
- **Proper and invertible fractional order ideals** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A fractional $\mathcal O$-ideal $I$ is *proper* if its multiplier order $\{a\in K:aI\subseteq I\}$ is $\mathcal O$, and it is *invertible* if $IJ=\mathcal O$ for some fractional $\mathcal O$-ideal $J$.
- **Ordinary ideal class group** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The ordinary ideal class group $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(\mathcal O)$ consists of proper invertible fractional $\mathcal O$-ideals modulo $I\sim aI$ for $a\in K^\times$.
- **Normalized arithmetic lattice** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For such an ideal put $$\label{eq:intro-lattice} \Lambda_{I,\sigma} =\mathop{\mathrm{covol}}(\sigma(I))^{-1/4}\sigma(I)\in X_4.$$
- **Volume-weighted packet probability** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Define $$\label{eq:intro-measure} \mu_{\mathcal O,\sigma} =\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal Y\in\mathcal P_{\mathcal O,\sigma}} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(\mathcal Y)\nu_{\mathcal Y}} {\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal Y\in\mathcal P_{\mathcal O,\sigma}} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(\mathcal Y)}.$$
- **Local unit-translate labels** ([source](#eq:pkt-local-choices)): Let $\mathscr L_{\mathcal O}(I)$ be the set of full lattices $L\subset I$ whose completions have the form $$\label{eq:pkt-local-choices} L_p=\xi_p t_p\mathcal O_p,\qquad t_p\in R_p^\times \quad\text{for every }p.$$
- **Local containment probability** ([source](#eq:ord-local-probability)): Choose $x$ in this shell and define $$\label{eq:ord-local-probability} P_p(\boldsymbol l_p) =\mathbb P_{t_p\in R_p^\times}\{x\in t_p\mathcal O_p\},$$ where the probability is normalized unit Haar measure.
- **Ordinary ideal weight** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For a nonzero integral $R$-ideal $\mathfrak a$, set $$\label{eq:ord-ideal-weight} P(\mathfrak a)= \prod_{p\mid q} P_p\bigl((v_{\mathfrak p}(\mathfrak a))_{\mathfrak p\mid p}\bigr).$$
- **Exterior quadratic form** ([source](#pm-h-17)): For $w\in\bigwedge^2\mathbb R^4$, write $w_{ij}$, $i<j$, for its coefficients in the basis $e_i\wedge e_j$, and define $$\label{eq:rig-Q} Q(w)=w_{12}w_{34}-w_{13}w_{24}+w_{14}w_{23}.$$
- **Exterior obstruction event** ([source](#eq:rig-events)): Define $$\label{eq:rig-events} \mathcal E_b =\left\{\Lambda\in X_4: \text{there exists }w\in\bigwedge\nolimits^2\Lambda\cap V_b \text{ with }Q(w)\ne0\right\}.$$
- **Quadratic exterior product map** ([source](#eq:ext-product-map)): Define the quadratic expression $$\Psi(w)=\delta_{\mathcal O}^{-1} (w_{12}w_{34},-w_{13}w_{24},w_{14}w_{23}). \label{eq:ext-product-map}$$
- **Weighted exterior multiplicity** ([source](#eq:ext-multiplicity)): For $z\in F$, define the nonnegative weighted multiplicity $$M(z)= \sum_{\substack{I,\; w\in(\bigwedge^2_{\mathbb Z}I\setminus\{0\})/U^+\\ z(I,w)=z}} \prod_p P_p(I,w). \label{eq:ext-multiplicity}$$
- **Stabilizer order and discriminant** ([source](#eq:ext-stabilizer-order)): $$C_F=\{\alpha\in F:\rho(\alpha)L_V\subset L_V\},\qquad \Delta_F=\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(C_F). \label{eq:ext-stabilizer-order}$$
- **Auxiliary order and trace dual** ([source](#pm-h-22)): Define the order and its trace dual $$C_F^\sharp=\mathbb Z+2C_F,\qquad (C_F^\sharp)^\vee =\{z\in F:\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{F/\mathbb Q}(zC_F^\sharp)\subset\mathbb Z\}.$$
- **Denominator ideal** ([source](#pm-h-25)): For $A=(A_\nu)\in E$, its denominator ideal is $$\mathfrak h_p(A)=\prod_\nu\mathfrak q_\nu^{h_\nu(A)},\qquad h_\nu(A)=\max\{0,-v_\nu(A_\nu)\},$$ where $v_\nu(0)=+\infty$.
- **Exterior logarithmic fiber weight** ([source](#eq:end-logarithms)): For a nonzero $z\in F$, set $$\label{eq:end-logarithms} \mathcal L_{L,r}(z) =\log_+\frac{L^2}{\|z_{b_1}\|}\, \log_+\frac{r^2}{\|z_{b_2}\|}\, \log_+\frac{r^2}{\|z_{b_3}\|}.$$

      "exact_definition": "For $z\\in F$, define the nonnegative weighted multiplicity $$M(z)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{I,\\;\n    w\\in(\\bigwedge^2_{\\mathbb Z}I\\setminus\\{0\\})/U^+\\\\\n    z(I,w)=z}}\n       \\prod_p P_p(I,w).\n \\label{eq:ext-multiplicity}$$",

          "exact_quote": "Define the stabilizer order and its discriminant by $$C_F=\\{\\alpha\\in F:\\rho(\\alpha)L_V\\subset L_V\\},\\qquad\n \\Delta_F=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Disc}}(C_F).\n \\label{eq:ext-stabilizer-order}$$",

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-order-discriminant-scale | Order discriminant | = | $q^2D=X^2$ | The field discriminant and order index may vary independently. |
| q-local-label-count | Number of local labels above a maximal-order ideal | = | $\prod_{p\mid q}[R_p^\times:\mathcal O_p^\times]$ | The count is independent of the chosen maximal-order ideal. |
| q-packet-total-volume | Total Haar volume of the distinct packet orbits | = | $N_{\mathcal O}\sqrt D\,\kappa$ | The Haar normalization and all sign components are those specified in the packet model. |
| q-shell-tail | Reciprocal-norm shell mass above the truncation | ≤ | $\frac Cq X^{-1/128}$ | Uniform over the field and order. |
| q-inverse-residue | Inverse Dedekind zeta residue | ≤ | $C\log(3D)$ | The constant is absolute, including bounded field discriminant. |
| q-weighted-ideal-interval | Weighted count of ideals in the prescribed norm interval | ≤ | $C\,\frac{\kappa y}{q}$ | The bound constant is absolute; the threshold depends on the fixed interval parameters. |
| q-cusp-mass | Limiting upper cusp probability | ≤ | $Cr^4$ | The radius is fixed before taking the limit superior. |
| q-limit-ball-mass | Probability-limit mass of a local ball | ≤ | $C_\Omega r^4$ | The centers lie in the specified compact set and the radius is in its permitted range. |
| q-dominated-entropy-lower | Entropy of a dominated invariant probability | ≥ | $\lambda_a/3$ | Domination is by a finite multiple of a probability satisfying the compact ball estimate. |
| q-global-coset-pairs | Global ideal-vector pairs in a fixed assignment of local cosets | ≤ | $32$ | The product value and local reference choices are fixed. |
| q-unramified-depth-tail | Probability of a prescribed minimum maximal component depth | ≤ | $3p^{-k}$ | Positive integer depth thresholds at unramified primes outside the exceptional set. |
| q-stabilizer-discriminant-lower | Stabilizer-order discriminant | ≥ | $\frac12 X^{1/2}$ | Uniform over the primitive field and arbitrary order. |
| q-stabilizer-discriminant-upper | Stabilizer-order discriminant | ≤ | $C X^{28}$ | The constant is absolute. |
| q-scalar-density-deviation | Absolute deviation of the scalar trace density from its constant main term | ≤ | $\frac1{p(p+1)}$ | Every integral local trace. |
| q-profile-cutoff-exponent | Global denominator-profile cutoff exponent | = | $371$ | Sufficiently large order scale with the real region and nonzero trace fixed. |
| q-nonscalar-saving-margin | Nonscalar power-saving exponent | > | $2\tau+3\theta$ | The box and modulus exponents are chosen together with the saving exponent. |
| q-exterior-box-limsup | Limit superior of the packet exterior-box vector average | ≤ | $C_{m,L}r^4$ | The trace and large-coordinate bound are fixed before the packet limit; the radius is also fixed during that limit. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-local-labels** [claim](#stmt-lem-pkt-local-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pkt-local-labels): Patch finite local unit quotients and characterize invertibility by local principality. -> Finite labels representing all invertible order classes, with independent local Haar probabilities.
- **step-packet-normalization** [claim](#stmt-prop-pkt-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pkt-model): Identify unit equivalences, count sign choices, and apply the class-number formula. -> The packet bijection, total orbit volume, and normalized integration formula.
- **step-ordinary-unfolding** [claim](#stmt-prop-ord-unfolding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ord-unfolding): Average local membership and unfold free unit orbits by nonnegative integration. -> A weighted integral-ideal sum retaining the packet normalization.
- **step-shell-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-ord-shells) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ord-shells): Compute shell Haar masses and use projection valuation tails to bound a positive moment. -> Exact shell mass and a uniform truncation tail.
- **step-residue-control** [claim](#stmt-lem-ord-residue) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ord-residue): Exclude exceptional zeros using primitivity and integrate the completed zeta logarithmic derivative. -> Uniform residue comparison, inverse-residue bound, and prime-sum control.
- **step-shiu-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-ord-shiu) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ord-shiu): Apply the short-interval theorem with uniform divisor-function growth bounds. -> The uniform multiplicative short-interval upper bound.
- **step-weighted-intervals** [claim](#stmt-prop-ord-weighted-intervals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ord-weighted-intervals): Separate conductor-supported norms, remove their Euler factors, and bound the shell tail. -> Uniform weighted ideal counts with the residue and inverse index factors.
- **step-tightness** [claim](#stmt-prop-ord-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ord-tightness): Integrate the cube fiber, sum weighted ideal intervals, and apply Mahler compactness. -> Tight packet probabilities and invariant probability subsequential limits.
- **step-vector-boxes** [claim](#stmt-lem-ord-boxes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ord-boxes): Bound logarithmic box fibers and the associated product-norm interval. -> The uniform vector-box average for each fixed small radius.
- **step-limit-balls** [claim](#stmt-prop-ord-balls) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ord-balls): Choose nonvanishing lattice vectors over compact charts and pass open neighborhood bounds to the weak limit. -> The compact ball-mass estimate for the limit.
- **step-tube-criterion** [claim](#stmt-thm-rig-tube-criterion) / [proof](#pm-h-15): Use the cited entropy criterion for two-sided tubes. -> An entropy lower bound for the limiting flow.
- **step-dominated-entropy** [claim](#stmt-prop-rig-dominated-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rig-dominated-entropy): Cover a two-sided tube by shrinking balls with centers in a compact diagonal enlargement. -> Positive entropy for every dominated invariant probability.
- **step-component-entropy** [claim](#stmt-cor-rig-positive-components) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-rig-positive-components): Restrict the ergodic decomposition to hypothetical zero-entropy components and apply the entropy integral formula. -> Positive entropy in almost every diagonal-ergodic component.
- **step-measure-classification** [claim](#stmt-thm-rig-ekl-classification) / [proof](#pm-h-16): Apply positive-entropy measure classification and convert the quotient convention by inversion. -> A closed finite-volume homogeneous supporting orbit.
- **step-equal-blocks** [claim](#stmt-lem-rig-equal-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rig-equal-blocks): Use unimodularity to balance root spaces, recover the rational commutant, and exclude nontrivial idempotents. -> Equal coordinate blocks and their totally real commutant field.
- **step-exterior-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-prop-rig-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rig-obstruction): Use a trace-zero commutant element to obtain a rational opposite-coordinate exterior plane. -> The Haar alternative or an exterior-plane witness with nonzero integer quadratic value.
- **step-product-map** [claim](#stmt-lem-ext-products) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ext-products): Descend signed products by Galois equivariance and use the exterior Pfaffian identity. -> A rational quadratic product map, integral trace form, and covariance.
- **step-fiber-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-ext-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ext-comparison): Factor the coordinate ratios over a splitting field and descend the unique projective tuple. -> A unique scalar and exterior field action modulo scalar ambiguity.
- **step-local-disintegration** [claim](#stmt-lem-ext-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ext-density): Use local submersion charts and compute the fiber form and compact unit-coset mass. -> An exact density formula for accepted local coset fractions, including conductor primes.
- **step-global-cosets** [claim](#stmt-lem-ext-global-cosets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ext-global-cosets): Compare globally, absorb a rational square, and bound remaining unit signatures and signs. -> A uniform finite bound on global ideal-vector pairs in the assignment.
- **step-unramified-data** [claim](#stmt-lem-ext-unramified-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ext-unramified-data): Count Frobenius-invariant valuation records and average geometric series on the trace coset. -> Local majorants, exact means, valuation tails, and residue dependence.
- **step-multiplicity-majorant** [claim](#stmt-prop-ext-multiplicity-majorant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ext-multiplicity-majorant): Sum over finitely many accepted coset assignments and multiply their local bounds. -> A finite global multiplicity majorant.
- **step-quadratic-module** [claim](#stmt-lem-ext-quadratic-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ext-quadratic-structure): Descend the plane-scalar action and polarize the split trace form. -> A self-adjoint resolvent-module action on a unimodular quadratic lattice.
- **step-stabilizer-scale** [claim](#stmt-lem-ext-order-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ext-order-scale): Clear embedding denominators for the upper bound and transfer a trace-dual projection denominator through exterior contraction for the lower bound. -> Polynomial upper and growing lower bounds for its discriminant.
- **step-product-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-ext-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ext-support): Use integral trace pairings, the trace Gram adjugate, and a nonzero field norm. -> Global trace-dual support and bounds on depth and real coordinate size.
- **step-abstract-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-fourier-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fourier-density): Express the product map by an algebra-valued bilinear form and use submersion regularity away from zero components. -> A locally constant density supported in the auxiliary trace dual.
- **step-scalar-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-fourier-scalar-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fourier-scalar-density): Integrate each split pair exactly and invert the absolutely summable scalar Fourier series. -> Exact scalar coefficients and a uniformly bounded positive trace density.
- **step-shell-shift** [claim](#stmt-lem-fourier-shift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fourier-shift): Avoid the forbidden scalar shifts componentwise and use the finite stabilizer quotient. -> An invertible shifted frequency with controlled inverse depth.
- **step-quadratic-transform** [claim](#stmt-lem-fourier-quadratic-transform) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fourier-quadratic-transform): Complete the square in the quadratic Fourier formula and normalize the Gaussian constant. -> The inverse quadratic integral and its isometry-invariant phase.
- **step-shell-estimates** [claim](#stmt-lem-fourier-shell-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fourier-shell-estimates): Combine inverse-operator Smith indices, translation periods, and residue-field quadratic Gauss sums. -> Uniform Fourier decay, inverse support, and boundary decay.
- **step-pointwise-inversion** [claim](#stmt-lem-fourier-inverse-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fourier-inverse-expansion): Invert on growing compact frequency balls and use inverse-support bounds to stabilize the shell sum. -> A finite pointwise inverse-shell expansion.
- **step-global-profiles** [claim](#stmt-lem-add-profile-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-add-profile-bounds): Multiply local inverse supports and boundary bounds, then compare the supporting ideal with a nonzero principal ideal. -> A bounded-norm finite profile expansion and a supporting fractional ideal.
- **step-cubic-lattice** [claim](#stmt-lem-add-ideal-lattice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-add-ideal-lattice): Use the nonzero ideal norm, a bounded-defect basis, and successive minima. -> Frequency point counts and bounded integral coordinates.
- **step-nonscalar-saving** [claim](#stmt-lem-add-nonscalar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-add-nonscalar): Separate field-discriminant and order-index regimes, then combine Fourier decay, inverse support, discriminant separation, and inverse congruences. -> An absolute nonscalar power saving with margin for box and modulus losses.
- **step-additive-count** [claim](#stmt-prop-additive-equidistribution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-add-nonscalar-2): Apply field Poisson summation, discard nonscalar frequencies, and evaluate the scalar series by trace-marginal Poisson summation. -> Uniform additive distribution in shrinking rectangles and permitted residue classes.
- **step-residue-functions** [claim](#stmt-lem-sieve-residue-function) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sieve-residue-function): Sum uniform residue-class estimates against nonnegative weights. -> A relative error retaining the local residue mean.
- **step-depth-assignment** [claim](#stmt-lem-sieve-assignment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sieve-assignment): Optimize a truncated Selberg upper-bound square and control its residue modulus and coefficient error. -> A joint upper bound preserving both assigned means and inactivity probabilities.
- **step-weighted-sieve** [claim](#stmt-prop-sieve-averaging) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sieve-assignment-2): Remove exceptional depths, stop at crossing prefixes, and sum Rankin-weighted prime buckets. -> The weighted unramified average bounded by the product of local means.
- **step-planar-count** [claim](#stmt-thm-exterior-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sieve-assignment-3): Majorize a rectangle smoothly, insert the local multiplicity bound, and compare the resulting Euler product with the zeta residue. -> The normalized planar exterior multiplicity bound.
- **step-exterior-unfolding** [claim](#stmt-lem-end-unfold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-end-unfold): Unfold free positive-unit orbits, retain both determinant signs, and compute the logarithmic-coordinate Jacobian. -> The exact exterior integral with logarithmic fiber weights.
- **step-exterior-boxes** [claim](#stmt-prop-end-tube) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-end-tube): Remove narrow coordinate strips and sum the remaining dyadic rectangles with their logarithmic weights. -> A radius-power bound for packet exterior boxes in the fixed-radius limit.
- **step-identify-haar-limit** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-end-tube-2): Pass open witness events to probability limits, shrink their transverse radius, and exclude proper homogeneous ergodic components. -> Every subsequential probability limit is Haar, giving full packet convergence and no escape of mass.

Its members satisfy $$[I:L]=q,\\qquad RL=I,$$ and are proper invertible fractional $\\mathcal O$-ideals.       "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-ord-shells",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ord-shells",
          "exact_quote": "With the preceding notation, $$\\label{eq:ord-shell-mass}\n \\sum_{\\boldsymbol l}\\frac{P(\\boldsymbol l)}{d(\\boldsymbol l)}\n       =\\frac{Z_q}{q}.$$ For every $\\eta>0$ there is a constant $C_\\eta$, independent of $K,\\mathcal O$, such that $$\\label{eq:ord-shell-moment}\n \\sum_{\\boldsymbol l}\n       P(\\boldsymbol l)d(\\boldsymbol l)^{-1+1/8}\n       \\le C_\\eta\\,\\frac{q^\\eta}{q}.$$ In particular there is an absolute constant $C$ for which $$\\label{eq:ord-shell-tail}\n \\sum_{d(\\boldsymbol l)>X^{1/8}}\n       \\frac{P(\\boldsymbol l)}{d(\\boldsymbol l)}\n       \\le \\frac Cq X^{-1/128}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }

      "id": "step-shell-control",

      "method": "Compute shell Haar masses and use projection valuation tails to bound a positive moment.",
      "output": "Exact shell mass and a uniform truncation tail.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-ord-shells"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-ord-residue",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ord-residue",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $D_0>1$, $h_0>0$, $Y_0>1$, and $C\\ge1$ with the following properties for every primitive totally real quartic field $K$.*\n\n\n1.  *If $D\\ge D_0$, then $$\\label{eq:ord-residue-comparison}\n     C^{-1}\\kappa\\le h\\zeta_K(1+h)\\le C\\kappa\n           \\qquad(0<h\\le4/\\log D).$$ If $D<D_0$, the same inequalities hold for $0<h\\le h_0$.*\n\n\n2.  *One has $$\\label{eq:ord-residue-lower}\n     \\kappa^{-1}\\le C\\log(3D).$$*\n\n\n3.  *Whenever $Y\\ge\\max\\{Y_0,D^{1/4}\\}$, $$\\label{eq:ord-prime-sum}\n     \\exp\\left(\\sum_{p\\le Y}\\frac{a_K(p)}p\\right)\n           \\le C\\kappa\\log Y .$$*\n\n*The sum in Equation <a href=\"#eq:ord-prime-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ord-prime-sum\">[eq:ord-prime-sum]</a> is over rational primes.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-residue-control",
      "inputs": [
        "A primitive totally real quartic field."
      ],
      "method": "Exclude exceptional zeros using primitivity and integrate the completed zeta logarithmic derivative.",
      "output": "Uniform residue comparison, inverse-residue bound, and prime-sum control.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-ord-residue"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-ord-shiu",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ord-shiu",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C,u_0$ such that, for every nonnegative multiplicative function $b$ with $b(n)\\le d_4(n)$, every real $u\\ge u_0$, and every $u^{1/2}\\le v\\le u$, $$\\label{eq:ord-shiu}\n \\sum_{u-v<n\\le u}b(n)\n \\le C\\frac v{\\log u}\n       \\exp\\left(\\sum_{p\\le u}\\frac{b(p)}p\\right).$$",

        }

          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for each fixed $A\\ge1$ and $c>0$, there is a threshold $X_0(A,c)$ with the following property. For every $K,\\mathcal O,\\sigma$ under consideration with $X\\ge X_0(A,c)$, and all real $x,y$ satisfying $$X^{3/4}\\le x\\le AX,\\qquad cx\\le y\\le x,$$ one has $$\\label{eq:ord-weighted-interval}\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\mathfrak a\\subset R,\\ \\mathfrak a\\ne0\\\\\n                  x-y<\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}\\mathfrak a\\le x}}P(\\mathfrak a)\n       \\le C\\,\\frac{\\kappa y}{q}.$$ The constant $C$ is independent of $A,c$; the threshold may depend on them.",

Consequently, for every sequence of these packet measures $\\mu_i=\\mu_{\\mathcal O_i,\\sigma_i}$ with $X_i\\to\\infty$ and every $\\epsilon>0$, there is a compact $\\mathcal K_\\epsilon\\subset X_4$ with $\\mu_i(\\mathcal K_\\epsilon)\\ge1-\\epsilon$ for all sufficiently large $i$.           "exact_quote": "Let $p\\notin S$. In any integral affine coordinates on the trace lattice, $\\{n_p\\ge k\\}$ is determined modulo $p^k$, and $b_p\\mathbf1_{\\{n_p=k\\}}$ is determined modulo $p^{k+1}$ for every finite $k\\ge0$.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ext-quadratic-structure",
          "exact_quote": "The plane-scalar action descends to a rational action $\\rho:F\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{End}}_{\\mathbb Q}(V)$ for which $V$ has rank two over $F$. The form $Q_V$ in an exterior $\\mathbb Z$-basis of $L_V$ is the three split pairs in Equation <a href=\"#eq:ext-integral-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ext-integral-form\">[eq:ext-integral-form]</a>; in particular $B_V$ is unimodular on $L_V$. The action $\\rho$ is self-adjoint for $B_V$, and for $\\alpha\\in F$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q_\\alpha(x):=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\alpha\\Psi(x))\n    &=\\tfrac12 B_V(\\rho(\\alpha)x,x), \\label{eq:ext-polar-operator}\\\\\n B_\\alpha(x,y)&=B_V(\\rho(\\alpha)x,y), \\notag\\\\\n \\det_{\\mathbb Q}\\rho(\\alpha)&=\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\alpha)^2,\\qquad\n \\Psi(\\rho(\\alpha)x)=\\alpha^2\\Psi(x). \\label{eq:ext-rank-two}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $B_\\alpha$ is the polarization of $Q_\\alpha$. The same assertions hold after base change to every $\\mathbb Q_p$.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }
      ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-ext-order-scale",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ext-order-scale",
          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\\frac12 X^{1/2}\\le\n \\Delta_F=D_F[R_F:C_F]^2\\le C X^{28}.\n \\label{eq:ext-order-scale}$$ More precisely, with $d=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Disc}}(\\mathcal O)=X^2$, $$d^2R_F\\subset C_F\\subset R_F,\\qquad D_F\\ll D^2.$$ In particular $C_{F,p}=R_{F,p}$ for $p\\nmid Dq$.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-stabilizer-scale",
      "inputs": [
        "The stabilizer order of the exterior lattice."
      ],
      "method": "Clear embedding denominators for the upper bound and transfer a trace-dual projection denominator through exterior contraction for the lower bound.",
      "output": "Polynomial upper and growing lower bounds for its discriminant.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-ext-order-scale"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-ext-support",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-stabilizer-scale"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ext-support",
          "exact_quote": "One has $$=4,\\qquad\n \\Delta_F^\\sharp:=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Disc}}(C_F^\\sharp)=16\\Delta_F\\ll X^{28},\n \\label{eq:ext-sharp-discriminant}$$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}:(C_F^\\sharp)^\\vee\\to\\mathbb Z$ is onto. For $p\\notin S$, $C_{F,p}^\\sharp=(C_{F,p}^\\sharp)^\\vee=R_{F,p}$. For every prime $p$, the density $f_p^*$ on $F_p^\\times$ is supported on $(C_{F,p}^\\sharp)^\\vee$. Consequently $$M(z)>0\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ z\\in(C_F^\\sharp)^\\vee.$$ The following stronger containment is used to average the majorant in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:ext-multiplicity-majorant\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:ext-multiplicity-majorant\">24</a>: if $z\\in F^\\times$ is integral outside $S$ and $\\prod_{p\\in S}f_p^*(z)>0$, then $z\\in(C_F^\\sharp)^\\vee$.*\n\n\n*Fix $m\\ne0$ and a bounded $\\Omega\\subset\\mathcal H_m$. Every $z\\in(C_F^\\sharp)^\\vee\\cap\\Omega$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\Delta_F^\\sharp z\\in R_F,\\qquad\n 1\\le|\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\Delta_F^\\sharp z)|\\ll_\\Omega X^{84},\n       \\label{eq:ext-norm-bound}\\\\\n &\\prod_{p\\notin S}p^{n_p(z)}\n       \\le|\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{F/\\mathbb Q}(\\Delta_F^\\sharp z)|,\\qquad\n |z_b|\\gg_\\Omega X^{-84}\\quad(b\\in\\mathcal B).\n \\label{eq:ext-depth-coordinate-bound}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constants are uniform in all arithmetic data and in the point of $\\Omega$.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }
      ],

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-ext-support"

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-density",
          "exact_quote": "The measure $\\mu_p$ has a density $f_p^*$ with respect to $dz$. It has a finite locally constant version at every $z=(z_\\nu)\\in E$ with $z_\\nu\\ne0$ for all $\\nu$. This version vanishes outside $$(\\mathcal C_L^\\sharp)^\\vee\n =\\{z\\in E:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{E/\\mathbb Q_p}(z\\mathcal C_L^\\sharp)\\subset\\mathbb Z_p\\}.$$ In particular $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{E/\\mathbb Q_p}(u\\Psi_E(x))\\in\\mathbb Z_p$ on the domain of Equation <a href=\"#eq:fourier-density-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-density-law\">[eq:fourier-density-law]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-abstract-density",
      "inputs": [

      "method": "Express the product map by an algebra-valued bilinear form and use submersion regularity away from zero components.",
      "output": "A locally constant density supported in the auxiliary trace dual.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-fourier-density"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-scalar-density",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-abstract-density"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-scalar-density",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that the integral scalar form is exactly $Q_1=x_1y_1-x_2y_2+x_3y_3$ as in Equation <a href=\"#eq:fourier-split-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-split-form\">[eq:fourier-split-form]</a>. Regard $a\\in\\mathbb Q_p$ as a scalar in $E$. In the quartic application this scalar form is $Q_1=Q_V$. Then, for every prime $p$, including $p=2$, $$\\label{eq:fourier-scalar-coefficients}\n \\gamma_p(a)=\n \\begin{cases}\n 1,&a\\in\\mathbb Z_p,\\\\\n p^{-3k},&v_p(a)=-k<0.\n \\end{cases}$$ The trace of the law in Equation <a href=\"#eq:fourier-density-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-density-law\">[eq:fourier-density-law]</a> has density $d_p(t)\\mathbf1_{\\mathbb Z_p}(t)$ for additive measure giving $\\mathbb Z_p$ mass $1$. This density is continuous on $\\mathbb Z_p$ and satisfies $$\\label{eq:fourier-scalar-density-bound}\n |d_p(t)-1|\\le\\frac1{p(p+1)}\\qquad(t\\in\\mathbb Z_p).$$ In particular $5/6\\le d_p(t)\\le7/6$, and for every finite set of primes $S$ and every choice $t_p\\in\\mathbb Z_p$, $$\\prod_{p\\in S}d_p(t_p)\\asymp1$$ with absolute upper and lower constants.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-scalar-density",
      "inputs": [

      "method": "Integrate each split pair exactly and invert the absolutely summable scalar Fourier series.",
      "output": "Exact scalar coefficients and a uniformly bounded positive trace density.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-fourier-scalar-density"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-shift",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-shift",
          "exact_quote": "Put $\\iota_p=1$ for $p\\le5$ and $\\iota_p=0$ for $p>5$. For every $A\\in\\mathscr S_{\\mathfrak h_p}$ there is an $s\\in\\{0,1,2,3\\}$ such that $\\alpha=A+s\\in E^\\times$ and $$v_\\nu(\\alpha_\\nu)=-h_\\nu\\quad(h_\\nu>0),\\qquad\n 0\\le v_\\nu(\\alpha_\\nu)\\le e_\\nu\\iota_p\\quad(h_\\nu=0).$$ Writing $\\beta=\\alpha^{-1}$, one has $$\\label{eq:fourier-shift-bounds}\n \\beta\\in p^{-\\iota_p}\\mathfrak h_p,\n \\qquad p^{-3\\iota_p}H_p\n       \\le |\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{E/\\mathbb Q_p}\\alpha|_p\\le H_p.$$ In particular the norm is exactly $H_p$ when $p>5$. If $$\\label{eq:fourier-j-definition}\n j_p=j_p(A,s)=\\min\\{j\\ge0:p^j\\beta\\in\\mathcal C_L\\},$$ then $j_p$ is finite and $j_p\\le g_p+\\iota_p$. Finally, for any generator $i\\in E^\\times$ of the ideal $\\mathfrak h_p$, the element $x=i(A+s)$ is in $R_E$, is a unit of $R_E$ if $p>5$, and satisfies $x^{-1}\\in p^{-\\iota_p}R_E$.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-shell-shift",

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-fourier-shift"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-quadratic-transform",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-quadratic-transform",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W_0$ be a finite-dimensional $\\mathbb Q_p$-space with a nondegenerate symmetric form $B_0$, and let $L_0\\subset W_0$ be a full $\\mathbb Z_p$-lattice self-dual for $B_0$. Use the self-dual Haar measure, which gives $L_0$ mass $1$. For an invertible $B_0$-self-adjoint operator $P$, put $q_P(x)=B_0(Px,x)/2$. There is a phase $\\omega(P)$ with $|\\omega(P)|=1$ such that $$\\label{eq:fourier-general-quadratic}\n \\int_{L_0}\\psi_p(q_P(x))\\,dx\n =\\omega(P)|\\det P|_p^{-1/2}\n       \\int_{L_0}\\psi_p(-q_{P^{-1}}(x))\\,dx.$$ The normalized phase is invariant under linear isometry of the quadratic form: $\\omega(T^*PT)=\\omega(P)$ for every invertible $T$, where $T^*$ is the $B_0$-adjoint.*\n\n\n*For the local data above, $\\alpha\\in E^\\times$, $\\beta=\\alpha^{-1}$ and $u\\in\\mathbb Z_p^\\times$, this gives $$\\label{eq:fourier-quadratic-transform}\n \\int_L\\psi_p(uQ_\\alpha(x))\\,dx\n =\\omega_p(u,\\alpha)|\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_{E/\\mathbb Q_p}\\alpha|_p^{-1}\n       \\int_L\\psi_p(-u^{-1}Q_\\beta(x))\\,dx,\n \\qquad |\\omega_p(u,\\alpha)|=1.$$ For every $r\\in E^\\times$ one has $\\omega_p(u,\\alpha r^2)=\\omega_p(u,\\alpha)$. All assertions hold at $p=2$.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }
      ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-shell-estimates",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-shell-estimates",
          "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be the cubic algebra above, let $W$ have rank two over $E$ with self-adjoint action for a nondegenerate symmetric form $B$, and let $L$ be self-dual for $B$, with $Q_1(L)\\subset\\mathbb Z_p$. Use the stabilizer order $\\mathcal C_L=\\{a\\in E:\\rho(a)L\\subset L\\}$ and $\\Psi_E,\\gamma_p$ from Equations <a href=\"#eq:fourier-polar-identity\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-polar-identity\">[eq:fourier-polar-identity]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:fourier-gamma\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-gamma\">[eq:fourier-gamma]</a>. Fix an integral ideal $\\mathfrak h_p=\\prod_\\nu\\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu}$, and use $H_p,g_p$ from Equation <a href=\"#eq:fourier-shell-indices\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fourier-shell-indices\">[eq:fourier-shell-indices]</a>. The zero-extended function $\\gamma_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}$ and its inverse $K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}$ are Schwartz–Bruhat functions. Moreover, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}\\subset(\\mathcal C_L^\\sharp)^\\vee\n \\subset\\{z\\in E:\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_{E/\\mathbb Q_p}z\\in\\mathbb Z_p\\}.$$ For $A\\in\\mathscr S_{\\mathfrak h_p}$ choose $s$ and $j_p=j_p(A,s)$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:fourier-shift\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:fourier-shift\">30</a>. Then $$\\label{eq:fourier-shell-decay}\n |\\gamma_p(A)|\\le p^{3\\iota_p}H_p^{-1}p^{-j_p/2},\n \\qquad\n \\|K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}\\|_\\infty\n       \\le p^{3\\iota_p}\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(R_E)\\le p^{3\\iota_p}.$$ Here $\\iota_p=\\mathbf1_{\\{p\\le5\\}}$, so the first constant is $1$ at every $p>5$ and is at most $125$ at the three remaining primes.*\n\n\n\n\n*If $g_p>0$ or $p\\le5$, then $$\\label{eq:fourier-inverse-support}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}\n       \\subset p^{-4(g_p+1)}\\mathfrak h_p\\mathfrak d_E^{-1}.$$ If $g_p=0$ and $p>5$, then support instead requires, for each $\\nu$, $$\\label{eq:fourier-boundary-support}\n z_\\nu\\in\n \\begin{cases}\n \\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1},&h_\\nu=0,\\\\\n \\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu-1}\\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1},&h_\\nu>0.\n \\end{cases}$$ For a $z$ satisfying these support conditions, define its set of boundary primes by $$\\partial_{\\mathfrak h_p}(z)\n =\\{\\mathfrak q_\\nu:h_\\nu>0,\n       \\ z_\\nu\\notin\\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu}\\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1}\\}.$$ In this case $$\\label{eq:fourier-boundary-decay}\n |K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z)|\n \\le 8\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(R_E)\n       \\prod_{\\mathfrak q_\\nu\\in\\partial_{\\mathfrak h_p}(z)}q_\\nu^{-1/2}.$$ Every constant is uniform in the lattice, the order $\\mathcal C_L$, the shell, and the field factors and their ramification.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }
      ],

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-fourier-inverse-expansion",
          "exact_quote": "If $z=(z_\\nu)\\in E$ has $z_\\nu\\ne0$ for every $\\nu$, then $$\\label{eq:fourier-inverse-expansion}\n f_p^*(z)=\\sum_{\\mathfrak h_p}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z),$$ where the sum is over integral $R_E$-ideals and only finitely many summands are nonzero.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-add-profile-bounds",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-add-profile-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "For every profile one has $$G_0\\le [R_F:C_F]\\le H G_0.\n \\label{eq:add-profile-indices}$$ Suppose that $z\\in F$ is integral outside $S$, its real image lies in $\\Omega_\\infty$, and $\\prod_{p\\in S}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z)\\ne0$. Define $$T_\\partial=T_\\partial(\\mathfrak h,z)\n   =\\prod_{\\substack{p\\in S\\setminus\\mathscr L_{\\mathfrak h}\\\\\n                     \\mathfrak q\\in\\partial_{\\mathfrak h_p}(z)}}\n       \\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}\\mathfrak q ,$$ where an empty product is one. There is a constant depending only on $\\Omega_\\infty$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n H&\\ll D_FG_0^{24}T_\\partial,\n &T_\\partial&\\le\\left(\\prod_{p\\in S}p\\right)^3,\n \\label{eq:add-profile-norm}\\\\\n \\left|\\prod_{p\\in S}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z)\\right|\n   &\\ll 8^{|S|}T_\\partial^{-1/2}.\n \\label{eq:add-profile-inverse}\n\\end{aligned}$$ All such $z$ lie in a fractional $R_F$-ideal $\\mathfrak a_{\\mathfrak h}$, independent of $z$, with $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}(\\mathfrak a_{\\mathfrak h})^{-1}\n       \\le 30^{12}D_FG_0^{24}.\n \\label{eq:add-profile-support}$$*\n\n\n*Set $C_3=371$. For all sufficiently large $X$, depending only on the fixed real data and $m$, a profile contributing at any such $z$ has $H\\le X^{C_3}$. For these $X$ and every $z\\in F$ integral outside $S$ with real image in $\\Omega_\\infty$, $$\\prod_{p\\in S}f_p^*(z)\n  =\\sum_{\\substack{\\mathfrak h=(\\mathfrak h_p)_{p\\in S}\\\\H\\le X^{C_3}}}\n        \\prod_{p\\in S}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z).\n \\label{eq:add-profile-expansion}$$ The number of profiles in this sum is $O_\\eta(X^\\eta)$ for every fixed $\\eta>0$, uniformly in the arithmetic data.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-add-ideal-lattice",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathfrak a$ be a fractional ideal of a totally real cubic field of discriminant $D_F$, and write $\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}(\\mathfrak a)=P^{-1}$ with $P>0$. Let $B_Y=\\{x\\in\\mathbb R^3:\\|x\\|\\le Y\\}$. In embedding coordinates it has covolume $\\sqrt{D_F}/P$, first Euclidean minimum $\\gg P^{-1/3}$, and, for $Y>0$, $$\\#\\{A\\in\\mathfrak a:\\|A\\|\\le Y\\}\n  \\ll 1+YP^{1/3}+Y^2P^{2/3}+\\frac{Y^3P}{\\sqrt{D_F}} .\n \\label{eq:add-lattice-count}$$ There is a $\\mathbb Z$-basis $b_1,b_2,b_3$ of $\\mathfrak a$ in which every $A=\\sum n_i b_i$ with $\\|A\\|\\le Y$ satisfies $$|n_i|\\ll YP^{1/3}\\quad(1\\le i\\le3).$$ All constants here are absolute.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-add-nonscalar",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute $\\tau,\\theta,\\epsilon_F>0$, with $\\epsilon_F>2\\tau+3\\theta$, such that for $C_3=371$ and the data of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:additive-equidistribution\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:additive-equidistribution\">34</a> with these $\\tau,\\theta$, $$\\sum_{\\substack{\\mathfrak h\\\\H\\le X^{C_3}}}\n   \\left|\n    \\sum_{A\\in F\\setminus\\mathbb Q}\n       \\widehat\\phi(A)\\widehat\\eta_{\\mathfrak h,l}(A)\n   \\right|\n     \\ll X^{-\\epsilon_F}.\n \\label{eq:add-nonscalar}$$ The constants have the same uniformity as in that proposition.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-additive-equidistribution",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-global-profiles",
        "step-nonscalar-saving",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-additive-equidistribution",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $\\tau,\\theta>0$ with the following property. Let $K,\\mathcal O,F$ be as in Section <a href=\"#sec:exterior\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:exterior\">5</a>, put $X=[\\mathcal O_K:\\mathcal O]\\sqrt{|\\mathop{\\mathrm{Disc}}(K)|}$, and take $S$ as in that section for the fixed nonzero integer $m$. In Equation <a href=\"#eq:add-box\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:add-box\">[eq:add-box]</a>, choose any two real partition coordinates and scales $$X^{-\\tau}\\le r_i,r_j\\le r_*$$ whose planar supports stay in the fixed bounded region above. Let $1\\le l\\le X^\\theta$ be prime to every prime in $S$. For each $p\\mid l$ choose any $z_p^0\\in\\mathcal T_{m,p}$, specifying a class modulo $p^{v_p(l)}$. Then $$\\sum_{\\substack{z\\in F,\\ \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}z=m\\\\\n                  z\\in R_{F,p}\\ (p\\notin S)\\\\\n                  z-z_p^0\\in p^{v_p(l)}R_{F,p}\\ (p\\mid l)}}\n       \\phi(z)\\prod_{p\\in S}f_p^*(z)\n =\n A_\\phi\\,l^{-2}\\prod_{p\\in S}d_p(m)\n       \\bigl(1+O(X^{-\\theta})\\bigr).\n \\label{eq:add-equidistribution}$$ Here $d_p$ is the trace density of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:fourier-scalar-density\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:fourier-scalar-density\">29</a>. The error is uniform over all the fields, orders, ordered real embeddings, permitted box locations and scales, moduli $l$, and chosen residue classes. The implied constant and the lower threshold for $X$ depend only on the fixed real data listed above and on $m$.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-packet-limit**: Volume-weighted complete packets converge to the invariant probability measure as the order discriminants tend to infinity, with no escape of mass.
- **invariant-local-label-average**: Independent local unit Haar probabilities induce the uniform distribution on the finite local labels representing invertible ideal classes.
- **invariant-compact-ball-control**: Every packet probability limit satisfies a uniform small-neighborhood mass bound on each compact set of centers.
- **invariant-dominated-entropy**: An invariant probability dominated by a finite multiple of a probability satisfying the compact ball bound has positive entropy for the specified diagonal flow.
- **invariant-homogeneous-obstruction**: A positive-entropy diagonal-ergodic probability is either the invariant probability of the full lattice space or is supported on an exterior-plane obstruction event.
- **invariant-admissible-exterior-rectangles**: The planar exterior multiplicity bound retains the packet normalization uniformly over admissible rectangles, orders, fields, and ideal representative systems.
- **invariant-split-scalar-marginal**: The exact integral split scalar form gives the stated scalar Fourier coefficients and uniformly bounded trace densities, including at the dyadic prime.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-individual-orbit-certification**: Treat an individual periodic orbit or a selected subset of ideal classes as an equidistributed packet. -> The counting arguments require the complete class average, and the theorem does not supply an individual-orbit conclusion.
- **anti-imprimitive-field-extension**: Apply the quartic packet theorem to fields containing a proper intermediate field. -> The residue argument uses primitivity to exclude the quadratic subfield forced by an exceptional real zero.
- **anti-arbitrary-ideal-module-type**: Replace invertible order ideals by arbitrary non-invertible ideals or unrestricted local module types. -> The local unit-translate parametrization describes invertible ideals, so the packet unfolding does not establish the same average for arbitrary modules.
- **anti-qualitative-limit-as-error-budget**: Use qualitative packet convergence as a finite-discriminant discrepancy certificate or a stopping rule for a numerical experiment. -> The main theorem asserts convergence and eventual compact concentration without a quantitative convergence rate for the packet measure.
- **anti-interchanging-radius-and-packet-limit**: Let the neighborhood radius shrink with the packet index using only the compact ball bound in probability limits. -> The vector-box threshold may depend on the fixed radius; the passage to the limit explicitly avoids interchanging these limits.
- **anti-arbitrary-exterior-slices**: Apply the planar exterior estimate at zero trace or to rectangles below its side-length cutoff. -> The statement fixes nonzero trace and imposes a minimum side length; the support and denominator arguments use nonvanishing field norms.
- **anti-general-quadratic-scalar-formula**: Use the exact scalar trace density for every integral self-dual quadratic lattice. -> The exact scalar coefficients use the integral split expression, while the shell estimates are stated under weaker hypotheses.
- **anti-primitivity-alone-excludes-limit**: Exclude proper homogeneous probability limits solely because the packet fields are primitive. -> The commutant field of a possible proper limit is not supplied with an embedding into the packet fields.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The engineering target is the packet equidistribution theorem, which concerns arbitrary sequences with growing order discriminant, convergence against every compactly supported continuous test function, and eventual compact concentration. Its proof identifies probability limits through ergodic decomposition, positive entropy, homogeneous measure classification, and exclusion of exterior obstruction events. The source states: “Every subsequential probability limit is now identified as $m_4$.” This is an infinite measure-theoretic conclusion rather than a finite constructive input-output result. Finite checks of the explicit quadratic identities would cover only auxiliary algebra and would not decide the packet limit or no-escape assertion.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The packet convergence theorem supplies an asymptotic mathematical guarantee but no software interface, finite stopping rule, or discrepancy tolerance for a program. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Tightness concerns arithmetic packet probabilities on a space of lattices. It does not construct a simulation sampler or establish finite sampling accuracy. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The explicit contraction of exterior endomorphisms provides an adaptable exact algebra checking construction: represent an endomorphism in a lattice basis, form its induced exterior operator, contract its coefficients, and compare with the stated trace identity. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The exterior trace form is an explicit polynomial in integral exterior coordinates. Evaluating its signed opposite products gives a finite exact arithmetic construction for the trace invariant. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The local labels form finite homogeneous spaces, but their mathematical parametrization does not specify a storage representation or operations with data structure guarantees. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The shell estimates bound local Fourier coefficients and inverse transforms over local fields; they provide no hardware mapping or accelerator resource model. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The comparison of product fibers characterizes algebraic equivalence under scalar and field actions. It establishes no cryptographic hardness, secrecy, or adversarial security property. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Positive entropy is established for invariant probabilities dominated by a measure satisfying the compact ball bound. The result supplies no learning procedure, training objective, or generalisation guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The weighted sieve controls arithmetic averages through prescribed depths and inactivity conditions. It does not formulate an optimisation problem or provide a solver contract. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Independent local Haar choices assemble arithmetic lattice conditions. This local-to-global construction supplies no distributed communication, consistency, or fault recovery protocol. |
| other (Algebraic number theory and arithmetic harmonic analysis) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs a rational action of the cubic resolvent on the exterior space and defines the stabilizer order by preservation of the exterior lattice. This gives a concrete mathematical framework for studying conductor dependent local quadratic densities. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-primitive-totally-real-quartic-fields** (hypothesis): The packet theorem requires primitive totally real quartic fields. It does not establish the same conclusion for fields with a proper intermediate field, nonreal embeddings, or another degree. Blocked use: ['Apply the packet equidistribution theorem to imprimitive quartic fields.', 'Transfer the theorem to other degrees without a separate argument.']
- **limit-unital-full-rank-orders** (hypothesis): The arithmetic order must be a full-rank subring containing the identity; an arbitrary lattice in the field does not satisfy the stated order hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Replace the order by an arbitrary additive sublattice when invoking the packet model.']
- **limit-invertible-ideal-local-type** (hypothesis): The local-label construction represents proper invertible fractional ideals. Arbitrary noninvertible ideals and unrestricted local module types lie outside this packet theorem. Blocked use: ['Use the invertible-ideal unfolding for arbitrary order modules.']
- **limit-complete-volume-weighted-packet** (hypothesis): Both counting arguments use the complete invertible ideal-class average with the specified orbit-volume weights and each distinct orbit counted once. Blocked use: ['Infer equidistribution for an individual periodic orbit.', 'Replace the complete packet by a selected collection of classes.', "Change the orbit weights while retaining the theorem's conclusion."]
- **limit-growing-order-discriminant** (hypothesis): The main conclusion requires the order discriminants to tend to infinity. The field may remain fixed, but a bounded order-discriminant family does not meet the limiting hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Claim eventual Haar distribution for a family whose order discriminants remain bounded.']
- **limit-qualitative-convergence** (nonconstructive): The main theorem identifies an asymptotic probability limit through compactness, ergodic decomposition, entropy, and homogeneous measure classification. A finite-discriminant discrepancy tolerance or convergence stopping rule is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Certify a numerical approximation error from the main convergence statement alone.', 'Treat finite algebraic checks as a decision procedure for packet equidistribution.']
- **limit-nonnegative-unfolding-and-residue-weights** (hypothesis): Ordinary-vector unfolding is stated for nonnegative Borel test functions and permits infinite values. The residue-function estimate also requires nonnegative weights so that its error retains the local mean. Blocked use: ['Assert finiteness from the unfolding identity without an additional bound.', 'Apply the residue-function relative error unchanged to signed weights with cancellation.']
- **limit-weighted-ideal-interval-regime** (hypothesis): Weighted ideal intervals must satisfy the stated norm and length ranges. The threshold depends on the fixed interval parameters even though the bound constant is absolute. Blocked use: ['Apply the weighted ideal bound to arbitrarily short intervals.', 'Treat the discriminant threshold as independent of the fixed interval parameters.']
- **limit-fixed-radius-and-compact-centers** (hypothesis): Ordinary vector boxes require compact centers away from the coordinate hyperplanes and a radius fixed before the packet limit. The resulting neighborhood bound is uniform on each specified compact set of lattice centers. Blocked use: ['Let the radius shrink with the packet index using only the probability-limit ball estimate.', 'Extend the compact-center constant to all lattice centers.', 'Allow vector-box centers to approach a coordinate hyperplane without further control.']
- **limit-probability-invariance-and-domination** (hypothesis): The tube criterion requires invariant probabilities and a probability weak limit. The dominated-entropy conclusion additionally requires finite domination by an invariant probability satisfying the compact ball bound, with the distinct flow weights specified in the entropy argument. Blocked use: ['Apply the entropy criterion to a limit that has lost probability mass.', 'Infer the dominated-entropy bound without finite domination.', 'Use the distinct-weight tube argument for a flow with coincident weights.']
- **limit-homogeneous-classification-is-not-haar-identification** (formal_scope): Positive-entropy measure classification requires diagonal invariance and diagonal ergodicity and yields a homogeneous orbit. In quartic degree a proper homogeneous alternative remains; field primitivity alone does not exclude it. Blocked use: ['Apply the classification theorem without diagonal ergodicity or positive entropy.', 'Identify a homogeneous component with Haar measure before excluding the exterior obstruction.']
- **limit-exterior-trace-and-rectangle-scale** (hypothesis): The planar exterior estimate fixes a nonzero integral trace and a bounded real region before taking the arithmetic limit. Both rectangle side lengths must meet the stated lower cutoff. Blocked use: ['Apply the planar estimate at zero trace.', 'Use rectangles below the permitted side-length cutoff.', 'Vary the trace or bounded real region without controlling their effect on constants and thresholds.']
- **limit-actual-conductor-density-and-local-prefactor** (formal_scope): At exceptional primes the multiplicity majorant uses the density defined by the actual exterior order lattice. The order index cancels from the disintegration prefactor but remains inside that density. Blocked use: ['Replace a conductor-prime density by its maximal-order counterpart.', 'Interpret cancellation in the local prefactor as independence from the order lattice.', 'Use unramified valuation factors at exceptional primes without their required local hypotheses.']
- **limit-quadratic-lattice-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The intrinsic shell estimates require the specified cubic algebra action, module rank, self-adjointness, self-duality, and integral scalar quadratic form. The sharper support and boundary estimates require the additional shell-index and prime conditions stated in the lemma. Blocked use: ['Apply the shell estimates to an arbitrary quadratic lattice.', 'Use the sharper boundary support at a small prime or for a shell outside the stated stabilizer-order regime.']
- **limit-exact-split-scalar-marginal** (hypothesis): The exact scalar Fourier coefficients and trace-density bounds require the exact integral split scalar form. Integrality and self-duality alone do not supply that scalar formula. Blocked use: ['Transfer the exact scalar marginal to every integral self-dual quadratic lattice.']
- **limit-nonvanishing-pointwise-inversion** (edge_case): Pointwise finite inverse-shell expansion is asserted only when every local output component is nonzero. The density need not be locally constant on the component-vanishing locus. Blocked use: ['Evaluate the finite pointwise shell expansion on an output with a vanishing local component.', 'Replace the cubic field by a product algebra in the global nonzero-norm truncation argument without a new justification.']
- **limit-additive-box-and-modulus-regime** (hypothesis): Additive distribution requires the prescribed smooth box family, permitted shrinking scales, fixed bounded real supports, and a modulus within the stated range prime to every exceptional prime. Its uniformity does not remove these restrictions. Blocked use: ['Impose an arbitrarily large residue modulus.', 'Include exceptional primes in the auxiliary modulus.', 'Use arbitrary discontinuous test functions in place of the specified smooth family without a justified approximation.']
- **limit-sieve-depth-inactivity-budget** (hypothesis): The prescribed-depth estimate requires disjoint active and inactive prime sets within the small-prime range and the stated depth-product budget. Retaining inactivity probabilities is necessary when local means fall below the constant main term. Blocked use: ['Assign conflicting active and inactive conditions at the same prime.', 'Apply the residue estimate to depth products exceeding its modulus budget.', 'Discard inactivity factors while claiming the same product of local means.']
- **limit-exterior-unfolding-signs-and-logarithms** (formal_scope): Exterior unfolding uses the same ideal representative system in the packet and multiplicity definitions, retains both signed trace slices, and includes the positive-unit index and logarithmic fiber factors. The exterior-box conclusion fixes its trace, large-coordinate bound, and radius during the packet limit. Blocked use: ['Drop one signed trace slice or the unit-index factor from the unfolding.', 'Replace logarithmic fiber weights by an unweighted multiplicity count without a bound.', 'Infer an exterior-box estimate uniform in simultaneously varying trace, coordinate bound, and radius.']
- **limit-incomplete-auxiliary-order-index-display** (formal_scope): The supplied trace-dual support lemma opens with an index display whose left-hand expression is missing. Its proof supplies a basis argument, but the incomplete displayed assertion must not be silently rewritten as a literal source statement. Blocked use: ['Publish a reconstructed index formula as an exact quotation of the supplied lemma.', 'Treat the malformed opening display as a complete independently readable identity.']

          "exact_quote": "Let $V_0\\subset(\\mathbb R^\\times)^4$ be compact, and let $b>0$. There are constants $C,r_0>0$, depending only on $V_0,b$, such that for every fixed $0<r<r_0$ there is a threshold $X_0(V_0,b,r)$ for which $$\\label{eq:ord-vector-box}\n \\int_{X_4}E_{\\mathbf 1_{v+[-br,br]^4}}\\,d\\mu_*\n       \\le Cr^4\\qquad(v\\in V_0,\\ X\\ge X_0(V_0,b,r)).$$ The threshold and constants are uniform over $K,\\mathcal O,\\sigma$ and over the centers $v\\in V_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-ord-balls",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be a weak probability limit of packet measures along $X_i\\to\\infty$.           "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-ord-balls",
          "exact_quote": "It uses no interchange between a shrinking radius and the packet limit.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        }

          "exact_quote": "Let $\\xi_i$ and $\\xi$ be $a(\\mathbb R)$-invariant Borel probabilities on $X_4$, with $\\xi_i$ converging weak-\\* to $\\xi$.           "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-rig-dominated-entropy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be an $A_4$-invariant probability on $X_4$ satisfying the compact ball bound in Equation <a href=\"#eq:ord-balls\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ord-balls\">[eq:ord-balls]</a> for every compact set of centers.           "source_id": "PM-015.2"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-15",
          "exact_quote": "#### Entropy from the compact ball estimate\n\nFix distinct real numbers $\\lambda_1,\\ldots,\\lambda_4$ with sum zero, and set $$a(t)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{diag}}(e^{\\lambda_1t},\\ldots,e^{\\lambda_4t}),\n \\qquad\n \\lambda_a=\\min_{i\\ne j}|\\lambda_i-\\lambda_j|>0.$$",

          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute $\\tau>0$ with the following property. Fix $m\\in\\mathbb Z\\setminus\\{0\\}$ and a bounded set $\\Omega\\subset\\mathcal H_m$. With $t=p^{-1}$ and $f_{\\mathfrak p}$ the residue degrees of the primes of $K$ above $p$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E_p b_p\n   &=(1-t)(1-t^3)\n       \\prod_{\\mathfrak p\\mid p}(1-t^{f_{\\mathfrak p}})^{-1}\n     =1+\\frac{a_K(p)-1}{p}+O(p^{-2}), \\label{eq:ext-unramified-mean}\\\\\n \\mathbb P_p(n_p\\ge k)&\\le3p^{-k}\\qquad(k\\ge1). \\label{eq:ext-unramified-tail}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The error constant is absolute. In any integral affine coordinates on the trace lattice, $\\{n_p\\ge k\\}$ is determined modulo $p^k$, and $b_p\\mathbf1_{\\{n_p=k\\}}$ is determined modulo $p^{k+1}$ for every finite $k\\ge0$.",

          "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be the cubic algebra above, let $W$ have rank two over $E$ with self-adjoint action for a nondegenerate symmetric form $B$, and let $L$ be self-dual for $B$, with $Q_1(L)\\subset\\mathbb Z_p$. Then $$\\label{eq:fourier-shell-decay}\n |\\gamma_p(A)|\\le p^{3\\iota_p}H_p^{-1}p^{-j_p/2},\n \\qquad\n \\|K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}\\|_\\infty\n       \\le p^{3\\iota_p}\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(R_E)\\le p^{3\\iota_p}.$$ Here $\\iota_p=\\mathbf1_{\\{p\\le5\\}}$, so the first constant is $1$ at every $p>5$ and is at most $125$ at the three remaining primes.*\n\n\n\n\n*If $g_p>0$ or $p\\le5$, then $$\\label{eq:fourier-inverse-support}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}\n       \\subset p^{-4(g_p+1)}\\mathfrak h_p\\mathfrak d_E^{-1}.$$ If $g_p=0$ and $p>5$, then support instead requires, for each $\\nu$, $$\\label{eq:fourier-boundary-support}\n z_\\nu\\in\n \\begin{cases}\n \\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1},&h_\\nu=0,\\\\\n \\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu-1}\\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1},&h_\\nu>0.\n \\end{cases}$$ For a $z$ satisfying these support conditions, define its set of boundary primes by $$\\partial_{\\mathfrak h_p}(z)\n =\\{\\mathfrak q_\\nu:h_\\nu>0,\n       \\ z_\\nu\\notin\\mathfrak q_\\nu^{h_\\nu}\\mathfrak d_\\nu^{-1}\\}.$$ In this case $$\\label{eq:fourier-boundary-decay}\n |K_{p,\\mathfrak h_p}(z)|\n \\le 8\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(R_E)\n       \\prod_{\\mathfrak q_\\nu\\in\\partial_{\\mathfrak h_p}(z)}q_\\nu^{-1/2}.$$ Every constant is uniform in the lattice, the order $\\mathcal C_L$, the shell, and the field factors and their ramification.",

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Let <i>K</i> range over totally real quartic fields with no proper intermediate field, and let $`\mathcal O`$ be any order in <i>K</i>. We prove that the packets of periodic diagonal orbits attached to invertible $`\mathcal O`$-ideal classes, weighted by orbit volume, equidistribute with no escape of mass as $`|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}\nolimits (\mathcal O)|`$ tends to infinity. The proof combines measure rigidity with a cubic-resolvent estimate that remains uniform at primes dividing the order index.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Ideal classes in a totally real number field give compact orbits of a diagonal group on a space of lattices. A natural arithmetic question is whether the finite collections of orbits obtained in this way become uniformly distributed as their discriminants grow. We prove this for the complete packets attached to invertible ideals of every order in a primitive totally real quartic field. The order may have arbitrarily large index in the ring of integers.

Let $$X_4=\mathrm{SL}_4(\mathbb Z)\backslash\mathrm{SL}_4(\mathbb R)$$ be the space of covolume-one row lattices in $\mathbb R^4$, and let $m_4$ be its invariant probability measure. Put $$H_4=\{u\in\mathbb R^4:u_1+u_2+u_3+u_4=0\},\qquad
 A_4=\{\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(e^{u_1},e^{u_2},e^{u_3},e^{u_4}):u\in H_4\}.$$ The group $A_4$ acts on $X_4$ by right multiplication. Its Haar measure is $du_1\,du_2\,du_3$, with $u_4=-u_1-u_2-u_3$.

A quartic field $K$ is *primitive* if it has no field strictly between $\mathbb Q$ and $K$. Let $K$ be primitive and totally real, let $\mathcal O\subseteq\mathcal O_K$ be an order, and fix an ordering $\sigma=(\sigma_1,\ldots,\sigma_4)$ of its real embeddings. Here an order is a full-rank subring containing $1$. A fractional $\mathcal O$-ideal $I$ is *proper* if its multiplier order $\{a\in K:aI\subseteq I\}$ is $\mathcal O$, and it is *invertible* if $IJ=\mathcal O$ for some fractional $\mathcal O$-ideal $J$. The ordinary ideal class group $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(\mathcal O)$ consists of proper invertible fractional $\mathcal O$-ideals modulo $I\sim aI$ for $a\in K^\times$.

For such an ideal put <a id="eq:intro-lattice"></a>$$\label{eq:intro-lattice}
 \Lambda_{I,\sigma}
   =\mathop{\mathrm{covol}}(\sigma(I))^{-1/4}\sigma(I)\in X_4.$$ A covolume-one lattice is represented here by any positively oriented basis; two such bases differ by an element of $\mathrm{SL}_4(\mathbb Z)$. Let $W_{\mathrm{all}}$ be the sixteen diagonal matrices with entries in $\{1,-1\}$. Choose one ideal in each ordinary class and form the finite set of distinct orbits $$\mathcal P_{\mathcal O,\sigma}
 =\{\Lambda_{I,\sigma}wA_4:I\text{ a class representative},\
                                      w\in W_{\mathrm{all}}\}.$$ A matrix in $W_{\mathrm{all}}$ acts on the underlying lattice, so a sign matrix of determinant $-1$ also gives a point of $X_4$. Using all signs makes the set independent of the chosen ordinary ideal representatives. Every orbit is compact by the unit theorem; Section <a href="#sec:packets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:packets">2</a> proves these assertions and describes the associated measure.

For an orbit $\mathcal Y\in\mathcal P_{\mathcal O,\sigma}$, let $\nu_{\mathcal Y}$ be its invariant probability and let $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(\mathcal Y)$ be its volume for the specified Haar measure. Define <a id="eq:intro-measure"></a>$$\label{eq:intro-measure}
 \mu_{\mathcal O,\sigma}
 =\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal Y\in\mathcal P_{\mathcal O,\sigma}}
              \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(\mathcal Y)\nu_{\mathcal Y}}
        {\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal Y\in\mathcal P_{\mathcal O,\sigma}}
              \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(\mathcal Y)}.$$ Each distinct orbit occurs once in these sums.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $(K_i,\mathcal O_i,\sigma_i)$ be any sequence in which $K_i$ is a primitive totally real quartic field, $\mathcal O_i$ is an order in $K_i$, and $\sigma_i$ is an ordering of its real embeddings. If $|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(\mathcal O_i)|\to\infty$, then $$\int_{X_4} f\,d\mu_{\mathcal O_i,\sigma_i}
       \longrightarrow \int_{X_4} f\,dm_4
       \qquad\text{for every }f\in C_c(X_4).$$ There is no escape of mass: for every $\varepsilon>0$ there is a compact $\mathcal K\subset X_4$ such that $\mu_{\mathcal O_i,\sigma_i}(\mathcal K)\ge1-\varepsilon$ for all sufficiently large $i$.*

</div>

In particular, the field may remain fixed while the order index tends to infinity. The packet in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> uses every class in $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(\mathcal O)$, so its ideals are locally free of rank one over the specified order. The proof uses this complete class average in both of its counting arguments.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and context

For real quadratic fields, compact diagonal orbits are closed geodesics on the modular surface. Linnik’s ergodic method and Skubenko’s theorem for positive discriminants established arithmetic distribution under the condition $\left(\frac d p\right)=1$ for the discriminant $d$ at a fixed odd prime $p$ (Linnik 1968; Skubenko 1962) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Linnik1968](#ref-Linnik1968), [Skubenko1962](#ref-Skubenko1962)]. Using Iwaniec’s estimates for half-integral weight forms (Iwaniec 1987) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Iwaniec1987](#ref-Iwaniec1987)], Duke removed that restriction for the geodesic collections associated with positive fundamental discriminants (Duke 1988, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Duke1988](#ref-Duke1988)]. Chelluri extended the corresponding statement to the unit tangent bundle, as recorded in (Einsiedler et al. 2012, sec. 1.2, p. 253) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ELMV2012](#ref-ELMV2012)]. Einsiedler, Lindenstrauss, Michel, and Venkatesh later gave a proof using entropy for all positive nonsquare order discriminants (Einsiedler et al. 2012, Theorem 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[ELMV2012](#ref-ELMV2012)].

In higher degree the diagonal group has higher rank, and the arithmetic packets lie in spaces of higher-dimensional lattices. Einsiedler, Lindenstrauss, Michel, and Venkatesh developed the discriminant and volume framework connecting separation of periodic torus orbits to entropy (Einsiedler et al. 2009) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[ELMV2009](#ref-ELMV2009)]. Their cubic theorem proves equidistribution of packets that include those arising from nonmaximal totally real cubic orders (Einsiedler et al. 2011, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[ELMV2011](#ref-ELMV2011)]. The local homothety class of a lattice is part of their packet data; a multiplier order alone need not specify it (Einsiedler et al. 2011, sec. 5.5) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[ELMV2011](#ref-ELMV2011)]. Their higher-dimensional discussion also identifies the possibility of proper intermediate homogeneous limits (Einsiedler et al. 2011, sec. 1.6.3) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[ELMV2011](#ref-ELMV2011)]. The theorem of Einsiedler, Katok, and Lindenstrauss makes a diagonal-ergodic probability with positive entropy homogeneous (Einsiedler, Katok, and Lindenstrauss 2006, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[EKL2006](#ref-EKL2006)]; in prime dimension at least three its homogeneous conclusion is already Haar (Einsiedler, Katok, and Lindenstrauss 2006, Corollary 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[EKL2006](#ref-EKL2006)].

For each fixed prime $p\ge5$, Lemke Oliver, Thorner, and Zaman proved equidistribution for the orbit collections attached to orders in totally real degree-$p$ fields as the order discriminant grows, outside an exceptional set containing $O_{p,\varepsilon}(Q^\varepsilon)$ fields of field discriminant at most $Q$ (Lemke Oliver, Thorner, and Zaman 2024, Theorem 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[LOTZ2024](#ref-LOTZ2024)]. The separate preprint (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[PrimeDegreePackets](#ref-PrimeDegreePackets)] treats every packet in each fixed prime degree at least five and permits arbitrary local module types. We adapt its ordinary-vector method in the argument below. For quartic packets, Khayutin obtained entropy bounds for probability weak-$*$ limits of maximal-order packet measures with two-transitive Galois groups, including the primitive quartic types $\mathfrak A_4$ and $S_4$ (Khayutin 2019a, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Khayutin2019Tori](#ref-Khayutin2019Tori)]. Wieser and Yang obtain entropy bounds for certain maximal-order quartic packets whose fields have a quadratic subfield, under further hypotheses (Wieser and Yang 2026) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[WieserYang2026](#ref-WieserYang2026)]. The present argument must establish tightness and positive entropy in almost every diagonal-ergodic component for arbitrary orders, and then exclude the proper homogeneous limits allowed in degree four.

The arithmetic use of a sieve to rule out intermediate algebraic limits has a precedent in Khayutin’s work on joint distributions of CM points (Khayutin 2019b) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Khayutin2019CM](#ref-Khayutin2019CM)]. Local Fourier analysis for nonmaximal orders already appears in the cubic work of Einsiedler, Lindenstrauss, Michel, and Venkatesh (Einsiedler et al. 2011, Proposition 9.9) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[ELMV2011](#ref-ELMV2011)]. Here the object being transformed is a specific quadratic product map on an exterior lattice. The resulting estimate controls arbitrary conductor depth in a cubic-resolvent count, before the remaining multiplicative weights are inserted by a sieve.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The two counts and the conductor estimate

Write $$\begin{gathered}
 R=\mathcal O_K,\qquad D=|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(K)|,\qquad q=[R:\mathcal O],\\
 X=q\sqrt D=\sqrt{|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(\mathcal O)|},\qquad
 \kappa=\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{s=1}\zeta_K(s).
 \end{gathered}$$ We also write $a_K(n)$ for the number of nonzero integral $R$-ideals of norm $n$. Both counting arguments preserve the factor $\sqrt D\,\kappa$ that normalizes the packet after its finite local labels are averaged.

Sections <a href="#sec:packets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:packets">2</a> and <a href="#sec:ordinary" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:ordinary">3</a> treat ordinary lattice vectors. An invertible $\mathcal O$-ideal is locally a unit translate of $\mathcal O$ inside a maximal-order ideal. Averaging those translates gives an exact unfolding into a weighted sum over integral $R$-ideals. A local moment bound controls the order index, while Stark’s exceptional-zero result and the absence of a quadratic subfield retain the residue $\kappa$ in the ideal count. Counting short vectors proves tightness. Counting vectors in boxes away from the coordinate hyperplanes gives an $O(r^4)$ bound for radius-$r$ neighborhoods in $X_4$, uniformly on each compact set of centers in every probability limit. For a diagonal flow with distinct weights, a two-sided tube is covered by $O(r^{-3})$ such neighborhoods with centers in a fixed compact enlargement, where $r$ decreases exponentially with the tube length. The resulting exponential decay also holds for normalized averages over sets of $A_4$-ergodic components with positive measure, because they are dominated by a multiple of the limit measure. This forces positive entropy in almost every component.

Section <a href="#sec:rigidity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:rigidity">4</a> applies measure classification and identifies the remaining obstruction. A proper positive-entropy component has two coordinate blocks of dimension two. Each lattice in its supporting orbit then has a vector in its exterior square supported on the two opposite coordinates $ij$ and $kl$, with both coefficients nonzero. There are three choices of the partition $ij|kl$.

For an exterior vector $v=(v_{ij})_{i<j}$, the positive diagonal action preserves the three signed products $$(v_{12}v_{34},\,-v_{13}v_{24},\,v_{14}v_{23}).$$ Their sum is an integer on the exterior square of a unimodular lattice. For the arithmetic lattices in our packets, Section <a href="#sec:exterior" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exterior">5</a> places the products in the three embeddings of a cubic resolvent field $F$. Fixing their sum at a nonzero integer $m$ puts them on a trace-$m$ plane. If the four exterior coordinates outside $ij,kl$ have size at most $r$, two product coordinates have size at most $r^2$. Thus the geometric obstruction is tested by a rectangle of area $O(r^4)$ on that plane. Theorem <a href="#thm:exterior-count" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:exterior-count">19</a> supplies the corresponding arithmetic rectangle bound, with the multiplicity of every product and the packet normalization included.

The main new work lies in proving that bound uniformly in $\mathcal O$. At primes dividing $Dq$, the difficult local factors in the multiplicity are given by the density of the pushforward of Haar measure on the exterior square of the order lattice, with a unit scalar, under the quadratic product map. Its exact fiber measure accounts for ramification and for the order index. An element of $F$ acts on each opposite-coordinate plane by the value of the corresponding real embedding; the resulting action descends to the rational exterior space $\bigwedge^2_{\mathbb Q}K$. The elements preserving $\bigwedge^2_{\mathbb Z}\mathcal O$ form an order $C_F$. Its discriminant is bounded below by a fixed positive power of $X$ and above by a fixed power of $X$, even if the field $F$ remains fixed.

Section <a href="#sec:local-fourier" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:local-fourier">6</a> analyzes these densities through their quadratic Fourier transforms. Denominator ideals organize the frequencies; the stabilizer order controls the decay of the Fourier coefficients and the support and size of the corresponding inverse transforms. The local proof is expressed in terms of the quadratic lattice, its action by a cubic étale algebra, and its stabilizer order, a formulation suitable for analogous cubic-resolvent counts. In Section <a href="#sec:additive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:additive">7</a>, Poisson summation gives the trace density from scalar frequencies. Complementary estimates control the total nonscalar contribution using discriminant separation, bounds for the Fourier and inverse transforms, and congruences supplied by inverse quadratic forms. The estimate is uniform for small residue moduli and for shrinking rectangles.

At the remaining primes, elementary valuation records give a local factor with mean $1+(a_K(p)-1)/p+O(p^{-2})$. Section <a href="#sec:sieve" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sieve">8</a> averages these factors by a weighted Selberg sieve. The subtraction of $1$ in the local mean removes the logarithm in the prime-sum bound, leaving $\kappa$. Finally, Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">9</a> unfolds exterior vectors along the diagonal group. The fiber lengths are logarithmic and summable over the rectangles above. The resulting $O(r^4)$ bound excludes every proper homogeneous component and proves Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Conventions

The notation $\mathop{\mathrm{N}}_{E/k}$ denotes the algebraic field norm. Ideal norms $\mathop{\mathrm{N}}(\mathfrak a)$ are positive absolute norms, extended multiplicatively to fractional ideals; absolute values are written explicitly for field norms when needed. For a rational prime $p$, a subscript $p$ denotes tensoring a field with $\mathbb Q_p$ or a lattice with $\mathbb Z_p$. For a prime $\mathfrak p$ or $\mathfrak q$ of a local field we use the integer valuation with value $1$ on a uniformizer, and write $e$ and $f$ for its ramification index and residue degree. A normalized extension of $v_p$ has $v_p(p)=1$, and $|p|_p=p^{-1}$. Ideal norms include the residue degrees. We use $\mathfrak p$ for primes of $K$ and $\mathfrak q$ for primes of $F$ when the two occur together.

All logarithms are natural, and $\log_+t=\max(0,\log t)$ for $t>0$. An upper bound $X^{o(1)}$ means $O_\eta(X^\eta)$ for every fixed $\eta>0$. Unless stated otherwise, constants are uniform in $K$, $\mathcal O$, and the ordering of the embeddings. In the exterior estimates a nonzero trace $m$ and a bounded real region are fixed before $X$ tends to infinity; permitted dependence on them is stated in the relevant results.

<a id="sec:packets"></a>
### Packets from arbitrary orders

Fix a primitive totally real quartic field $K$, an order $\mathcal O\subset K$, and an ordering $\sigma=(\sigma_1,\ldots,\sigma_4)$ of its real embeddings, as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. We describe the packet using finitely many local lattice choices above each ideal class of the maximal order. This description lets us average a containment condition at each finite prime before performing an integral at the real place. The construction is adapted from the packet model in (OpenAI 2026, sec. 2, Lemma 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[PrimeDegreePackets](#ref-PrimeDegreePackets)]; we give the argument with all sixteen sign patterns in degree four.

<a id="eq:pkt-covolume"></a>
Throughout the paper put <a id="eq:pkt-scale"></a>$$\label{eq:pkt-scale}
 R=\mathcal O_K,\qquad D=|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(K)|,\qquad q=[R:\mathcal O],\qquad
 X=q\sqrt D,\qquad \kappa=\mathop{\mathrm{Res}}_{s=1}\zeta_K(s).$$ In particular, $|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(\mathcal O)|=q^2D=X^2$. Absolute ideal norms are extended multiplicatively to fractional $R$-ideals. Thus $$\label{eq:pkt-covolume}
